C-45To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?A683rd of 26full answer under 4a Business quality
P-04

After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?

A652nd of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: price is the steadiest positive driver in the model. Developer-licence list prices rise 5-10% a year, and I expect realised price of about +4-5% a year after seat optimisation and discounting, which contributes about 3 points of group revenue growth every year and, because it costs nothing to deliver, more than pays for wage inflation: net of 3% wage inflation it adds about 1.5 points of margin a year at flat volume. Royalties contribute no price (fixed unit prices, no indexation), and IAR's conversion contributes a one-off uplift of 1.5-2.5 times per converted customer that runs through 2027-2028. Capacity, usage and compliance-based pricing is a genuine second lever but contributes nothing before 2028 in my base case.

The developer-licence escalator (about 60-65% of group revenue). Evidence of application, not conjecture: 5% list increases in 2024 and a similar target in 2025 (Carnegie, 14 February 2025); 5-10% on one-year contracts (DNB Carnegie); the three-year discount cut from 33% to 10% (a one-off effective increase of about 35% on three-year renewals, already in the base); no rise in churn through 2025, the weakest year in a decade; no customer lost in Q3 2025 (DNB Carnegie, 31 October 2025). The price came with a cost: customers bought the minimum seat count, shifted to one-year terms and delayed large deals, and small and mid-sized customers left after earlier large increases (expert account, 2023). So realised price per seat rises about 5%, seat volume moves between +2% and -3% depending on the cycle and AI, and developer revenue grows about price plus seats plus new programmes.

Arithmetic of the contribution (my base case). Developer, maintenance and consulting revenue excluding IAR of about EUR 159 million (2026) growing 8.0% in 2027 and 7.5-8% thereafter: about 4.5 points of that is price, about 1 point net seats, about 2-2.5 points new customers, testing (Squish, Axivion, Coco growing about 15%) and compliance-driven conversions. Price therefore adds about EUR 7 million of revenue in 2027, rising to about EUR 9-10 million a year by 2030, at close to 100% incremental margin. Wage inflation of about 3% on EUR 120-130 million of personnel cost costs EUR 3.6-4 million a year. The spread, about EUR 3-5 million a year, is the "price-over-cost" engine that lets margins rise even in a year with flat volume; it explains a large part of why 2023 and 2024 margins rose while seat volumes softened.

Royalties (about 20% of 2026 revenue). Unit prices are fixed for the life of a design, with no inflation pass-through and no volume discounts, so royalties lose about 2-3% a year in real terms and their growth is volume alone (design wins reaching production, P-05). Arm's experience (royalty per smartphone from USD 0.76 to USD 1.63 through Armv9 and Compute Subsystems) shows a royalty vendor can raise rates only on new architectures; QT has no equivalent lever disclosed, though Qt 6.12's compliance documentation could support higher rates on new designs over time. I model zero royalty price.

IAR (about 14% of 2026 revenue). Converted customers pay 1.5 to 2.5 times their previous maintenance payment (IAR, Q1 2025 report); PTC reports a 1.5-2.5 times ARR lift on SaaS migration; the Industry digest records Temenos term licences about 30% more and ATOSS licence-to-cloud 30-40% more. Subscription bookings reached 68% of IAR bookings in Q2 2026 (target 40%) and a majority in Q3. Because subscription revenue is recognised over the term, the uplift appears as revenue growth in 2027-2028 rather than in bookings; I model IAR at EUR 33.5 / 38.5 / 44.3 / 48.7 / 52.6 million (2026-2030), below the FactSet segment consensus of EUR 39.6 and 47.4 million for 2027-2028 because renewal of converted customers is unproven (the cloud offer launched in March 2025, so the first renewals of multi-year subscriptions are still ahead).

The second lever: capacity, usage and compliance pricing. IAR already charges on a combination of capacity and seats (Q3 2026 interim statement); the group plans to test capacity-based pricing with new customers in selected markets; management told analysts not to model revenue from it. Peers show it works when it is bundled (Atlassian's credits pooled across seats; TeamViewer's endpoint pricing carrying an ARR premium) and fails when it is retroactive and opaque (Unity's runtime fee, cancelled in September 2024 after a CEO departure and a 25% workforce cut). For QT the design choice matters: shared productivity gains will be accepted; a disguised price rise on a developer base already frustrated by enforcement would not. I give it zero in 2026-2028 and treat it as upside of perhaps 1 point of growth a year from 2029 if pilots convert.

What could break the escalator. (1) A customer response to AI that reduces seats faster than price rises (P-05); (2) a TeamViewer-style need to pause increases to stop small-customer churn (TeamViewer halted price increases in its SMB segment in 2026 after attrition near 2% a quarter, Deutsche Bank, 7 May 2026); (3) a strong euro, since US dollar price lists do not adjust for currency. None is visible in 2026: developer licences grew in Europe in Q3 2026 with more mid-sized and large wins.

Where I differ from the first set. I agree with its +5% price and 0 to -3% seat assumption for weak years; in a recovery year I allow net seats to be slightly positive. I disagree with the first set's (and DNB Carnegie's) emphasis on a duration-driven renewal tailwind: management's Q3 2026 comment that the shift to one-year licences has not substantially changed means the effect is already in the run-rate, and only a reversal back to three-year contracts would move reported revenue (upward, temporarily; see P-05).

5b

Operating leverage

2 answers
C-7555P-0162
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

C559th of 26

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: very material. About 85-90% of the cost base is fixed in the short run (mostly people), incremental revenue carries an 80-90% margin, and management adjusts costs only with a lag of about a year. At 2026-2027 margins, each 1% of revenue moves EBITA by about 3-4%, and the plausible 2027 range is roughly EUR 68-90 million of EBITA (a 26-32% margin) around a consensus of about EUR 79 million.

Cost structure: personnel is about 64% of operating costs, plus IT, premises and sales costs; variable costs (consulting delivery, hosting, commissions) are small. Inderes' sensitivity: one point of extra revenue growth adds about EUR 2.6 million of EBIT (about 3% of 2027 EPS); a two-point volume swing moves EBIT by about EUR 5.2 million.

The 2025 precedent shows the leverage working in reverse and the reaction function: the company planned for 15-25% growth and hired accordingly (headcount 858 in late 2024 to 958 on average in 2025), revenue grew 3.5%, and EBITA fell 27% (EUR 71 million to 52 million) as the margin dropped 10 points. The profit warning came in October 2025; the reorganisation was launched in April 2026; the savings arrive in 2027. The lag is therefore about 12-18 months, and the cost of acting (EUR 9.7 million) about half a year's savings.

Mix leverage: royalties carry close to 100% margin, so each EUR 5 million swing in royalties (about 10% of the line) moves the group margin by about 1.5-2 points. Q4 concentration adds to this: a slipped Q4 deal moves the full-year margin.

The 2027 range for the model: revenue EUR 255-280 million (brokers range from Inderes' 261 to DNB Carnegie's 287) and a cost base of about EUR 182-192 million, depending on how much of the EUR 20 million of savings is reinvested and how much was already in the 2026 run-rate, gives EBITA of roughly EUR 68-90 million and EPS of about EUR 1.65-2.25 (brokers' after-amortisation basis). Operating leverage is the main reason the cone of outcomes is wide even though the cost savings are largely secured.

P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

B+623rd of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: operating leverage will shape the next four years in two different ways at two different speeds. Within any one year it is very high, because about 85-90% of costs are fixed and a missed euro of licence revenue loses 85-90 cents of EBITDA; across years it is only moderate, because management has always reinvested growth into headcount, so each extra euro of revenue has historically added only 49-59 cents of EBITDA. The 2027 margin step-up is mostly the unwinding of the 2025 negative leverage plus self-help, not revenue leverage. After 2027, I expect margins to climb about 1.5 points a year, from 30% EBITDA in 2027 to about 34.5% in 2030, regaining the 2024 peak (35.7%) only at the end of the horizon. The cone of outcomes is skewed: the downside is fast and full (in-year), the upside is slow and partial (reinvested).

What the leverage is. Personnel costs were EUR 111 million of EUR 165 million of operating costs in 2025 (67%, AlphaSense cost report, from the 2025 accounts). Hosting, commissions and consulting delivery are small; the economic gross margin on licences is above 80% and near 100% on royalties. Costs are adjusted only through hiring decisions and, as 2025-2026 showed, through a restructuring with a lag of 12-18 months (profit warning 17 October 2025, reorganisation launched 20 April 2026, full savings in 2027).

How it behaved over the last four to six years (FactSet operating EBITDA).

Period Revenue change EBITDA change Incremental EBITDA margin What it shows
2021 to 2022 +34 +12.3 36% Hiring for growth absorbs most of the leverage
2022 to 2023 +26 +12.8 49%
2023 to 2024 +28 +16.4 59% Best year: fixed costs grew 12% against revenue +15.7%
2024 to 2025 +7 -19.3 negative Costs built for 15-25% growth met 3.5% growth: the margin fell 10 points

The pattern matters for the model: in the up-cycle, management spent between 40% and 65% of incremental revenue on new costs. Consensus 2028 implies an incremental EBITDA margin of 67% (EBITDA 84.6 to 98.0 on revenue 264 to 284), above anything the company has delivered in the last five years. I use 55% for 2028-2030, the middle of the 2023-2024 range.

The 2026 to 2027 bridge (my figures, underlying EBITDA). 2026 EUR 61.5 million; plus about 16.5 from revenue growth of EUR 18.6 million at about 90% gross contribution; plus about 11 of savings not yet in the 2026 cost base (I estimate about EUR 9 million of the EUR 20 million was captured in Q2-Q4 2026); less about 3.6 of wage inflation (3% on about EUR 120 million of personnel cost); less about 4 of reinvestment in APAC sales and AI research (APAC headcount already rose from 169 to 203 in the year to Q3 2026); less about 1.5 of bonus normalisation (no bonus was earned for 2025); less about 1 of commissions and IAR growth costs; equals about EUR 78.8 million, a 30.0% margin. Roughly 11 of the 17 points of EBITDA growth come from cost actions already taken, which is why the 2027 margin is more predictable than the 2027 revenue.

The real 2027 debate is a EUR 5 million question. Consensus EBITDA of EUR 84.6 million on revenue of EUR 264 million needs 2027 operating costs about 1.5% below 2026; mine are about 1% above. The difference (EUR 5-6 million, about 2 points of margin, about EUR 0.18 of EPS) is almost entirely whether management reinvests a quarter of the savings or almost none. Management's own words on the Q2 and Q3 2026 calls (savings "should not be read as a direct cut in next year's cost base") support my side; its "about 30% operating margin in 2027" (EBITA, which on my figures is 27.8%) supports consensus. Precedents from the AlphaSense cost report point the same way as management's caution: Truecaller's 20% staff-cost cut lifted adjusted EBITDA back above 30% in H2 2026 but RedEye notes part was offset by growth investment; Yubico's SEK 95 million saving lifted the EBIT margin to 25.3% by Q3 2026 but DNB Carnegie's 2028 forecast (17%) sits below management's target, because reinvestment absorbs part of it.

How it shapes each line over the horizon.

  • Revenue: leverage does not change it; cost cuts in sales could slow it (the US team was cut and rebuilt).
  • EBITDA and EBITA: margin path 25.2% / 30.0% / 32.0% / 33.5% / 34.5% (EBITDA) and 23.0% / 27.8% / 29.9% / 31.4% / 32.5% (EBITA), against 32.2% (2023), 35.7% (2024) and 25.6% (2025) EBITDA. The four-year view is a return to the 2023-2024 structure, not a new, higher one, because growth is about half its 2019-2024 rate and leverage is shared with reinvestment.
  • EPS: adjusted EPS grows 43% in 2027 and about 12-21% a year after that; about two thirds of the 2028-2030 EPS growth comes from operating leverage on 7-8% revenue growth.
  • Quarterly shape: Q4 carries about 32% of annual revenue and about 37-40% of annual EBITDA in my model (Q4 2025: 36% of revenue). Because costs are spread evenly, a EUR 5 million slip of a Q4 deal costs about EUR 4.5 million of annual EBITDA, almost 2 points of annual margin.

Sensitivities on the 2027 base (my arithmetic). One point of organic revenue growth: EUR 2.6 million of revenue, EUR 2.2-2.4 million of EBITDA within the year (about 3% of EPS). A 10% move in royalties (about EUR 5.2 million): EUR 4.5-5 million of EBITDA, about 1.8 points of margin, about 7% of EPS. A 10% move in the dollar: about EUR 9.5 million of revenue and EUR 5.5 million of EBITA (US dollar costs offset only about EUR 3.5-4 million), about 8% of EPS. A swing of EUR 5 million in reinvestment: 1.9 points of margin, about 7% of EPS.

The asymmetry, which is the insight for the third set. The cost base protects nothing in a shortfall (2025: revenue EUR 7 million above 2024, EBITDA EUR 19 million below) but shares the upside in a beat, because management hires into strength. So the cone around my 2027 EBITDA is wider below than above: a shortfall of 5 points of growth would take EBITDA to about EUR 67 million (27%) within the year, and a bear case of 2% organic growth, royalties 15% lower and a dollar 10% weaker to about EUR 62-65 million; an equal beat would add about EUR 11-12 million in the year, but on the 2022-2024 pattern only about EUR 7 million of it would persist into the next year's margin once hiring catches up. I would put the 2027 EBITDA range at EUR 64-88 million around my EUR 78.8 million, with the downside tail longer.

Where I differ from the first set. The first set centred 2027 EBITA at 28-29% and a range of EUR 68-90 million, which I share. Two refinements: the leverage is time-asymmetric (above), and Q4 2026 consensus appears to under-use the leverage already in the cost base (consensus implies Q4 2026 operating costs up about 10% on Q4 2025, or 6-7% after allowing for IAR's extra half-month, although headcount at end-September 2026 was about 12% below end-2025). My Q4 2026 margin of 35.5% assumes some normalisation of bonuses and commissions after a 2025 in which no bonus was earned; that is my hypothesis, not a disclosed fact, and it is the main reason I am not higher still.

5c

Financial leverage

2 answers
C-7670P-0266
C-76How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?C+707th of 26full answer under 4c Financial profile
P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

B+663rd of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: financial leverage is a small, fading and then reversing influence: it subtracts about EUR 0.19 of 2026 EPS, about EUR 0.10 in 2027 and almost nothing in 2028, after which the balance sheet turns to net cash and interest becomes income. The material balance-sheet question for 2028-2030 is not leverage but idle cash: on my base case net cash reaches about EUR 118 million by end-2029 and EUR 200 million by end-2030 (about a quarter of today's market value), so capital allocation, not gearing, becomes the swing factor for EPS from 2029.

The starting point. At 30 September 2026: bank loan EUR 120 million (EUR 150 million drawn for IAR, EUR 15 million repaid in H1 2026), leases EUR 6.6 million, cash EUR 50.2 million, net debt EUR 76.5 million, 1.3x trailing EBITDA (FactSet), equity ratio 53.6%. The loan is from Nordea with periodic amortisation (AlphaSense EPS report, citing the Q3 2026 presentation); its margin, tenor and covenants are not disclosed. I assume a floating cost of about 3.8% (three-month Euribor about 2% plus a margin of about 1.75%), which is consistent with the nine-month interest burden.

The path (my base case, no buybacks, no dividends, no acquisitions). Free cash flow after leases of EUR 32 / 50 / 64 / 75 / 82 million (2026-2030) takes net debt to EUR 71 million at end-2026, EUR 21 million at end-2027 and net cash of EUR 44 million at end-2028. Net financial items: EUR -6.3 million (2026, including foreign-exchange revaluation losses of the first half), -3.1 (2027), -0.5 (2028), then +1.2 (2029) and +2.5 (2030) as cash earns about 1.5%.

EPS effect. The change in net financial items adds EUR 3.2 million pre-tax in 2027 and EUR 2.6 million in 2028, about EUR 0.10 and EUR 0.08 per share. Against consensus, the gap is large in 2028: consensus pre-tax profit sits EUR 4.0 million below consensus EBIT in 2028 (and EUR 4.2 million in 2027), which implies financial costs barely falling while the loan is repaid; on my path that is EUR 1.1 million too high in 2027 and EUR 3.5 million in 2028 (about EUR 0.03 and EUR 0.11 per share). This is one of the few consensus errors that is close to arithmetic rather than judgement, conditional only on the company repaying debt, which it has said is its priority.

Amplification and risk. Gearing amplifies little: even in my bear case (2027 EBITDA about EUR 64 million), net debt to EBITDA at end-2027 would be about 0.6x. Leverage would exceed 2x only if EBITDA fell about 40% from 2026, which needs a royalty collapse, flat organic growth and a much weaker dollar at the same time. The Risks report's covenant scenario rested on depressed 2025-2026 earnings; at 2027 earnings it is remote. The balance sheet therefore adds almost no risk to the 18-month forecast.

The balance sheet from 2028: three paths with very different EPS outcomes.

  1. Cash accumulates (my base case, by default). Interest income of EUR 1-2.5 million a year; ROE dilutes; EPS as modelled.
  2. Buyback. The AGM authorisation covers 2 million shares (about 8%). At EUR 35-45 a share that costs EUR 70-90 million, affordable from end-2028 cash, and would add about 8% to EPS; QT bought EUR 11 million of its own shares in 2022, so the tool has been used, though only to manage the share count.
  3. Bolt-on acquisitions (management's stated preference, and the chief executive's record). EUR 100-200 million of deals in 2029-2030 would add revenue and new amortisation and could restart the pattern of buying growth at five times revenue (IAR). The first set (C-64) rightly expects this path; it would raise reported growth but lower the predictability and the return on capital. For the third and fourth sets, path 3 is the most likely and the least modelled: no broker builds acquisitions, so consensus growth for 2029-2030 is organic, and the first deal would add reported growth and amortisation that no forecast carries.

Other balance-sheet lines. Goodwill and acquired intangibles (EUR 114.2 million of identified intangibles from IAR; technology 45.4, customer relationships 38.7, trademarks 5.8) amortise at about EUR 12.5 million a year, so equity rises by about net income plus nothing returned: consensus equity of EUR 237 / 287 / 350 million (2026-2028) is consistent with my path. Working capital rises with revenue (P-08).

Cost-out scope NTM

C-7760P-0368
C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

B+603rd of 26

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: after the 2026 reorganisation the obviously cuttable layer (duplicate administration after IAR, the IAR security unit, Finnish roles moved to cheaper hubs) has been taken; a second round of perhaps EUR 10-20 million (5-10% of the cost base) would be possible in a downturn, but only by cutting sales and R&D capacity that the strategy needs. The cost base is reducible, with a one-year lag and severance of about half a year's saving.

By part:

  • Personnel (about 64% of operating costs): cut by up to 200 roles (about 18%) in 2026, including 56 in Finland and the US done by June. Further cuts are possible in sales (the US is underperforming) and in support functions as systems are merged. Nordic and European labour rules make cuts slow (the Finnish negotiations took about two months).
  • R&D (15-20% of sales): the most protected line; management kept R&D through 2025 and Clate, AI assistants and Qt for MCUs depend on it. Cutting it would weaken the moat.
  • Sales and marketing: reducible through the move to direct enterprise-account management and the rebalancing towards defence, medical and APAC; partly being reinvested.
  • Administration and IT: duplicate overhead from IAR (dual systems, entities, offices) is being removed; management expects further minor synergies.
  • External services and other: small (EUR 5.3 million of subcontracting in 2025).

The question for 2027-2028 is not whether more can be cut but how much of the EUR 20 million is reinvested. Management has signalled reinvestment in APAC sales and AI research, so the net saving is likely to be smaller than gross.

P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

A681st of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: about EUR 23 million of gross annual savings have been secured (EUR 20 million from the reorganisation plus EUR 3 million from closing IAR's embedded-security unit), and roughly EUR 16-18 million of that will stay in profit after reinvestment. About EUR 10 million, including most of the security saving, was already in the 2026 cost base (Q2-Q4), so the incremental net benefit to 2027 underlying EBITDA, after the increase in reinvestment, is about EUR 7-9 million, plus the end of EUR 10.5 million of restructuring charges, which lifts reported (not underlying) EBITA. After 2027 nothing material is left to cut without damaging the growth engine: only EUR 1-2 million of further integration synergies (systems, entities, offices) in 2027-2028. Cost elimination is therefore a 2026-2027 event with a high degree of certainty, not a recurring driver.

What has been done and what it shows. Up to 200 roles cut (about 18% of the pre-cut workforce): Finland concluded in Q2 2026 (56 roles), the United States by June, other European countries through late 2026. Headcount fell from 1,120 (end-March 2026) to 1,034 (end-June) and 1,002 (end-September). The evidence that savings are already flowing: on my reconstruction, underlying operating costs excluding IAR were about 3-4% lower year on year in Q1-Q2 2026 and about 11-12% lower in Q3 2026 (Q3 2026 underlying costs EUR 40.7 million on the EBITA basis against EUR 36.4 million in Q3 2025, with about EUR 8.5-9 million of IAR costs that were absent a year earlier). Restructuring costs: EUR 9.7 million in January-September, about half a year's saving, in line with Nordic precedents (Truecaller SEK 23.5 million for a 20% staff-cost cut; Yubico SEK 21 million for SEK 95 million of annual saving).

The EUR 3 million question, resolved. The first set could not say whether the IAR security closure was inside the EUR 20 million. The AlphaSense cost report, citing the H1 2026 report, states it lowers costs by EUR 3 million a year "in addition to" the programme, with little or no revenue effect. I treat gross savings as EUR 23 million. A small caution: the closure was decided in Q2 2026, so most of its benefit is in the 2026 run-rate already.

Timing: how much reaches which line, and when (my estimates).

Item 2026 2027 2028 Line affected
Gross savings captured in the year (programme plus security unit) about 10.5 about 23 about 23 Underlying EBITDA
Reinvestment (APAC sales, AI research, testing sales) about 1 about 5-6 about 6-7 Underlying EBITDA

Incremental to the prior year +9.5 +7.5-8.5 about 0
Restructuring charges -10.5 about 0 0 Reported EBITA and EPS only
Restructuring cash paid about -7.5 about -3 0 Free cash flow
Integration synergies (systems, entities, offices) small +1 +1 Underlying EBITDA

Against the 2026 base the net incremental gain in 2027 is about EUR 7.5-8.5 million (the 11 of gross programme savings not yet in the 2026 run-rate, plus about 1 of security saving, less about 4 of extra reinvestment; the same figures appear in the P-01 bridge); against reported figures, reported EBITA rises by about EUR 27.5 million from 2026 to 2027 (EUR 45.5 million to EUR 73.0 million), of which about 10.5 is the end of restructuring charges, about 8 is net savings and about 9-10 is revenue leverage net of wage inflation, bonus normalisation and other cost growth. About two thirds of the reported EBITA rise is therefore self-help that is already executed, which is the single most important fact for the predictability assessment.

How long comparable programmes took to show, and how much was delivered. The AlphaSense cost report's Nordic precedents show savings arriving within one to two quarters of the end of negotiations (Truecaller from Q3 2026 after Q2 cuts; Yubico from Q2 2026 after Q1 cuts), and QT's own Q3 2026 shows the same. Large European programmes take longer (SAP's 2024 restructuring of 8,000 roles, EUR 2 billion of cost, slow benefit), but QT's is a headcount cut, not a re-skilling programme. The recurring pattern across the precedents is that savings are delivered but partly reinvested; none of the named peers let the full saving reach profit.

What else is cuttable. After the reorganisation, a second round of EUR 10-20 million (5-10% of the cost base) would be possible only by cutting sales and R&D capacity the strategy needs (R&D at 15-20% of sales; testing, APAC and US sales). That is a downturn lever, not a base-case item. Finnish, Swedish and German labour rules lengthen any further round (Finnish negotiations took about two months; German works councils require social plans), so a second round would again take 12-18 months to reach profit.

Where I differ from the first set. Mostly agreement. Two refinements: the security saving is additional; and the reported EBITA jump in 2027 contains EUR 10.5 million of "saving" that is purely the end of a charge, which consensus EBITDA (before restructuring) does not count but reported EPS growth does, so headline reported EPS growth in 2027 (my +120%, from EUR 0.82 to EUR 1.80) will look much larger than the underlying improvement (+43% on the adjusted basis).

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4764P-0556
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?B646th of 26full answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

C+566th of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

The Investment Analyst's heading After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: the loops in QT's model mostly move slowly and roughly offset each other in revenue, so they should not move the centre of the 2027-2028 forecast; what they do is widen the cone and add two timing effects that a model built on smooth growth rates will miss. The one fast loop is accounting, not demand: upfront recognition of about 95% of multi-year contract value turns any change in contract length into a revenue swing, which amplifies the cycle in both directions. The reinforcing loops (developer standard plus AI training data, regulation, design wins to royalties, IAR cross-selling) favour 2028-2030 more than 2027; the balancing loops (price-driven funnel leakage, AI seat compression) are slow and mostly invisible until 2029 and later. On balance the loops support my 7-8% growth from 2028 but do not justify a return to double-digit organic growth.

Each loop, its direction, its timing and its effect on the forecast lines:

  1. Upfront recognition and contract length (fast, amplifying, two-sided). About 95% of a developer contract's value is recognised at signing. In 2025, customers shortened contracts from three years to one, which cut recognised revenue and released contract assets (free cash flow was 123% of net income). The loop runs the other way in a recovery: if confident customers return to three-year deals, revenue is pulled forward (a one-year contract worth 100 becomes a three-year contract worth 270 at the 10% multi-year discount, of which about 255 is recognised at signing), reported growth overshoots, working capital rises, and two years later the cohort leaves an air pocket. PTC's Q4 fiscal 2025 shows the same mechanism (average contract length rising from two to three years pulled forward revenue under ASC 606). Today management says the mix is stable, so I model no pull-forward. The forecast consequence: a strong 2027-2028 reported growth figure accompanied by rising receivables should be read as duration, not demand, and it raises 2029-2030 risk. Monitoring signal: contract assets (EUR 9.5 million at Q3 2026 against EUR 6.4 million a year earlier, already rising) and receivables against revenue.

  2. Design wins to royalties (reinforcing, three-to-five-year lag, held back by volume). Design wins of 2022-2025 (Chinese electric-vehicle programmes, the Suzuki e Vitara cockpit, consumer-electronics wins) reach production in 2027-2029 and add royalties without sales cost. But royalties are price times volume, and volume is weak: S&P Global (via DNB Carnegie, 3 November 2026) has global light-vehicle production -2% in 2026 and +1% in both 2027 and 2028; Jefferies expects consumer electronics to grow 11% in 2027 after a 22% fall in late-2026 smartphone shipments. Management expects royalties flat or slightly lower over the medium term. Consensus has royalties +11% in each of 2027 and 2028 (EUR 57.8 million and EUR 64.2 million). I model +2% in 2027 and +4% a year after, so the loop adds about EUR 1-2 million a year of near-100%-margin revenue rather than the EUR 6 million a year consensus carries. This is the largest single line where my model and consensus differ; it accounts for about EUR 5 million of 2027 revenue and EUR 4.5 million of 2027 EBITDA.

  3. Developer standard and AI training data (reinforcing, slow). About 1.5 million developers make Qt the default; specialist rivals cannot find developers (Hyundai executive, 2022); Qt's open-source code and documentation are well represented in AI training data, so coding agents write Qt well, and Clate (beta October 2026, full release early 2027) generates QML from designs. The loop protects price and share; it does not add measurable revenue in the horizon.

  4. Price, the open-source funnel and the low end (balancing, slow, lagged). Price increases and licence enforcement push small projects to LVGL and Slint (expert accounts 2022-2023; GigaDevice now supports both Qt for MCUs and LVGL on its chips), which shrinks the pool of future commercial conversions and, three to five years later, royalties. No metric measures it; developer forums show no shrinkage. Its financial effect, if any, lands after 2029 and on royalties. I model no explicit effect but hold royalty growth at 4%, below the market's content growth.

  5. AI: testing demand against developer seats (both directions, low confidence). AI multiplies code volume and the need for deterministic verification (Squish, Axivion, Coco; Axivion's NVIDIA CUDA rules); it also raises output per engineer. Rough arithmetic: testing revenue of about EUR 40 million in 2026 growing 15% adds about EUR 6 million a year; a 1-3% annual seat drag on about EUR 150 million of seat-based revenue removes EUR 1.5-4.5 million a year. Net: roughly neutral to +1 point of group growth. Morgan Stanley data (junior QA tester postings -7%, senior AI builder postings +103% in 2026) suggest the seat effect falls first on junior roles. Rothschild & Co Redburn's October 2026 hands-on tests suggest agentic stacks could let whole teams switch platforms within 12 months, which is the fast-path risk. The loop does not move my centre; it is the main structural reason the 2029-2030 cone is wide.

  6. Regulation: a dated pulse that reverts (reinforcing until December 2027, then partly reversing). The Cyber Resilience Act applies fully from December 2027; the Industry digest's rule is that a forced migration converts at a price premium and that the demand pulse reverts once the date passes. Conversions of open-source users and compliance-driven testing purchases should be pulled into 2027 (I allow about EUR 1-2 million inside developer growth) and leave a tougher comparison in 2028. The DNB Markets evidence (October 2024) that security tooling takes several years to commercialise argues against a large wave. Effect: a small, front-loaded 2027 benefit.

  7. IAR at chip selection (reinforcing, unmeasured). IAR's compilers are chosen when the chip is chosen, which gives leads for Qt for MCUs and testing into IAR's base of about 30,000 developers. There is no disclosure of cross-sold revenue. I include it only through testing growth.

  8. Credibility (reinforcing in both directions). Floor guidance, two quarters of beats and six months of upward revisions (2027 EPS +46%) have rebuilt trust; one slipped Q4 would reverse the loop and the multiple, whatever the full-year numbers. This loop works on the share price more than on the forecasts.

  9. Cash (reinforcing, mild). Deleveraging lowers interest, which raises free cash flow and speeds deleveraging; once debt is gone (2028), the loop turns into the capital-allocation question in P-02.

Net effect on the lines. Revenue: loops 2, 5 and 6 net to about +1 point of growth in 2027-2028 against a no-loop baseline, which is inside my 7.6-8.0%; loop 1 is an unmodelled two-sided timing risk of ±3-5% of a year's revenue. Margin: loop 2 matters most because royalties carry near-100% margin. Working capital: loop 1. The loops do not support consensus's +11% royalty growth, and they do not support DNB Carnegie's 15% organic growth.

Where I differ from the first set and from the AlphaSense loops report. The report concludes that the contract-renewal loop "will provide a highly predictable tailwind for net sales through 2028"; management's own Q3 2026 statement (no substantial change in the one-year shift) contradicts the premise, and the report itself records that contrary evidence. I also give the upfront-recognition loop more weight than the first set did: it is the mechanism by which the next upturn will overstate demand, as the 2021-2023 boom did (distribution +49.5% in 2023 when customers launched together).

Guidance is now a conservative floor that carries credibility but no information about the upside; the useful guidance is informal and points to a 2027 margin of about 30%, which consensus already holds. C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C557th of 26

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: for 2026, guidance is below consensus (slightly on revenue, clearly on margin), by design; for 2027 there is no formal guidance, and management's informal margin talk (about 30%) is in line with consensus. The gap exists because management moved to floor-only guidance after two cuts in 2025, not because it expects less than consensus.

2026: guidance of constant-currency growth of at least 10% (at least EUR 237.9 million) and an EBITA margin of at least 15% (at least EUR 35.7 million) compares with consensus revenue of EUR 243 million (+12.4%) and an implied reported EBITA of about EUR 42 million (about 17%; about 21% before restructuring). Revenue guidance is 2% below consensus; margin guidance is roughly 15% below on EBITA. In February 2026, when it was set, the guidance was about 43% below the consensus of the time, which disappointed the market (Inderes called it "a disappointment"). Since then, consensus has come down to near the floors and results have run above them.

2027: no formal guidance. Management's statement that 2027 will be "substantially better", with an operating margin of about 30%, matches consensus EBITA of about EUR 79 million (about 30% on EUR 264 million). It is not a commitment: in the same Q3 call management said savings would partly be reinvested, which, unless revenue beats, points to the lower end of the range.

Why: after missing its own targets in 2022 and 2025, management chose to publish only minimum levels "to avoid consecutive downgrades if market conditions soften", which the brokers welcomed.

C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

B+624th of 26

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: the 2026 guidance is very conservative: IAR alone adds about 11 points of growth in 2026, so the 10% floor would be met with roughly zero or slightly negative organic growth, against organic growth of 16-18% in 2023-2024 and 2.6% in 2025. The informal 2027-and-beyond ambitions (an operating margin of about 30% and long-term organic growth of about 10%) are realistic rather than conservative once the weaker input forces are accounted for.

2026: IAR contributed about EUR 8 million in Q4 2025 and should contribute about EUR 33 million in 2026 (segment consensus), so about EUR 25 million of the EUR 22 million needed to reach the 10% floor comes from consolidation alone. The margin floor of 15% compares with an underlying nine-month margin of about 18% and a Q4 that is normally the strongest quarter (Q4 2025: 35.5%).

2027-2029: an operating margin of about 30% compares with 30.2% (2023) and 34.1% (2024), so it is a return to history, helped by EUR 20 million of savings. Long-term organic growth of about 10% compares with about 29% a year in 2019-2024. Adjusted for change forces, the past was inflated by a demand pulse (2021-2023 programme launches, distribution +49.5% in 2023) and by price restructuring (subscription conversion, discount cuts); the future has weaker Western automotive, AI seat pressure and lower-cost competition at the low end, but also stronger compliance demand, testing and IAR. Net, about 10% organic growth is a fair mid-cycle estimate, not a sandbag; 2027 consensus (+8.5% total, about 6-7% organic) is below it, which is reasonable for a recovery year.

To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement: informal commentary is well above formal guidance and roughly in line with consensus for both years; within it, the bullish themes (2027 margin, AI, the Cyber Resilience Act, Q4 strength) are balanced by three cautions that point slightly below 2027 consensus: royalties flat or lower, savings partly reinvested, and no revenue from new pricing.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

C508th of 26

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: five uncommon features matter: floor-only guidance in constant currency on an EBITA basis; publication of the sell-side consensus before each report; a coverage base of only four to six mostly Nordic brokers, one of which is paid by the company; a new commitment to quarterly ARR and royalty disclosure alongside a refusal to give segment or organic figures; and an extreme Q4 weighting.

  1. Floors in constant currency, on EBITA. Guidance excludes currency (which moved reported growth by 3 points in 2025) and acquired-intangible amortisation (about EUR 12.5 million a year), so reported EPS can diverge from guided performance.
  2. Published consensus. The company publishes the Modular Finance consensus (six brokers) before results, which makes beats and misses unambiguous.
  3. Coverage. FactSet shows four brokers (Inderes, OP, Nordea, DNB Carnegie); Inderes is "commissioned research", paid by the company, and is also the highest on EPS (partly because it excludes amortisation). Consensus is thin and Nordic; no large international house covers the stock, which limits the buyer base and adds to sentiment risk.
  4. Disclosure. ARR and quarterly royalties are new and useful; organic growth, IAR and testing revenue remain undisclosed, and DNB Carnegie has asked for contract-maturity and segment data.
  5. Seasonality. Q4 2025 was 36% of 2025 revenue and about half of 2025 EBITA; Q4 2026 decides whether the floors are comfortably beaten.

The next report is due on 18 February 2027 (FactSet), with 2027 guidance (still, presumably, floors).

C-74

To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

A662nd of 26

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: yes on all counts. Estimates were cut deeply and across every broker in 2025 and early 2026 (2026 EPS down about 30% over twelve months, target prices down 65% at DNB Carnegie and 68% at Inderes from peak), guidance was rebased to floors that IAR alone almost meets, and 2026 was used to absorb restructuring and the IAR revenue trough. Estimates have since risen for six months, mostly on costs. The setup is a low base that has already started to be revised up.

Evidence:

  • Estimates: FactSet mean EPS for 2026 fell from a high of EUR 3.22 to a low of 0.905 and is now 1.17 (-30% over twelve months, +30% over six months); 2027 from 4.24 to a low of 1.36, now 1.99 (-13% over twelve months, +46% over six months); 2028 is 8.5% below its level of twelve months ago. Revenue estimates for 2027 are 9% below twelve months ago.
  • Targets and ratings: DNB Carnegie cut from EUR 110 (February 2025) to 50 (October 2025) and 38 (February 2026); Inderes from about EUR 100 to 32 before raising to 38 and 41; no broker moved to Sell; the buy share is 29%, down 14 points in the last month (Nordea at Hold).
  • Guidance rebased: from 15-25% growth (early 2025) to 3-10% (October 2025) to a floor of 10% including IAR (February 2026), which needs no organic growth.
  • Kitchen-sinking: EUR 9.7 million of restructuring booked in 2026, IAR's revenue deliberately depressed by a fast subscription conversion, and floor guidance described by management as cautious.

What it means: the downgrade cycle is complete and the upgrade cycle is six months old. The recent upgrades are cost-led (2027 EPS +46% but revenue only +5% over six months), which makes them durable if savings are delivered and fragile to any revenue disappointment. The dashboard's sell-side earnings momentum letter is B (as at 10 November 2026), with a revision-momentum score of 11.

C572nd of 6

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for the next 18 months are moderately predictable: more than the earlier sets' numbers implied and more than QT's record suggests. The part of the forecast that carries the change (the cost base, price, the end of a charge, the recognition of IAR's subscriptions) is demonstrated, traceable and in management's hands. The part that is not predictable (the level and timing of fourth-quarter revenue, royalties, the dollar) decides the spread around the forecast, not its direction.

The seven answers point the same way:

  • The earlier work is coherent, and its one material flaw has been corrected (D-01). The thesis is told consistently from industry structure through to the inputs. The numbers for the next two quarters were built on a misread consensus basis and on fourth-quarter costs set 28-31% above the third quarter. Corrected, my 2027 EBITDA (EUR 82.4 million), consensus (EUR 84.6 million) and management's "about 30%" lie within 3% of each other.
  • The change relies on what the business already does (D-02). About 80% of the 2027 rise in reported EBITA needs only price, executed cost cuts, IAR's recognition mechanics and the end of restructuring, and it implies revenue per employee only about 5% above 2024.
  • The drivers are concentrated in a helpful way for 2027 and an unhelpful way for any single year's variance (D-03). Three self-help items equal about 93% of the 2027 EBITA rise; one quarter earns about 42% of the year's EBITDA.
  • The cone is about 27% down and 15% up on 2027 EPS, and it stays open on revenue until February 2028 (D-04).
  • The case can be monitored well on costs and poorly on demand (D-05, D-06). The first-quarter 2027 report fixes the cost run-rate; nothing the company publishes separates price, seats, duration, testing and IAR.
  • The share price is QT's own; the level of its multiple is the sector's (D-07).

My steer to the memo:

  1. Present the 2027 margin (EBITDA about 31%, EBITA about 29%) as the predictable core of the case. Present 2027 revenue as a central estimate with an error of about 4-5%, and say that the error is a property of upfront recognition and December signings.
  2. The question for the next six months is not whether the margin arrives. It is whether growth excluding IAR is nearer 4% or 7% in the first half of 2027, because that decides the quality of the next round of estimate changes and whether any premium returns to the multiple.
  3. Two reports matter most: 18 February 2027 (the cost base, royalties, the 2027 floors, the reinvestment) and February 2028 (the fourth quarter of 2027). The first-quarter 2027 report matters for growth.
  4. On riskiness: the case is simple for 2027 (few drivers, mostly internal) and its fragilities are specific: fourth-quarter concentration, an unhedged dollar worth about 9% of EPS per 10%, and a royalty line that behaves like lumpy deals. Bad news has arrived suddenly in the past (profit warnings in October 2025 and in the first days of January 2024 and 2025). Floor guidance removes the trigger for a formal warning. It does not remove the shortfall against consensus that such a warning used to announce.
  5. Three developments would lower predictability inside the horizon: a return of customers to three-year contracts (revenue would overshoot and leave a gap two years later); an acquisition once net debt is gone, around the end of 2027; and any move of the installed base to capacity pricing.

One caution about what this score means. A forecast can be predictable without being different from what others expect. On the corrected numbers ours sits on consensus, and that is the starting point for the fourth set.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

C+607th of 26

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: narrow by business line (embedded software development tools, reported as one segment), broad by customer, industry and geography (more than 3,500 customers, 70 industries, Europe about 40%, North America 34%, APAC 25%). The perimeter widened modestly with IAR. Predictability of the base is good; predictability of each quarter is poor because of upfront recognition, Q4 concentration and royalties.

The breadth of customers and industries protects against any single failure (no customer above 10%; top ten a minor share), and the narrow product focus keeps the business understandable. IAR adds a related business line (compilers) sold to the same developers through the same chip partners, so the perimeter widened without diversifying the risk: all three product families depend on customers' R&D budgets.

Predictability is reduced by: (1) about 95% upfront recognition on developer licences, which converts contract-length decisions into revenue swings; (2) the fourth quarter, which carries the year (Q4 2025 was 36% of 2025 revenue); (3) royalties tied to production; (4) the absence of segment disclosure, which forces analysts to infer IAR, testing and organic trends; (5) currency. ARR, now published quarterly, is the most predictable line and should anchor the model.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

C+702nd of 6

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the expected change is plausible, and for 2027 it rests almost entirely on strengths the business has already shown. About 80% of the rise in reported EBITA from 2026 to 2027 needs only price increases that have been applied every year, cost cuts that are already in the run-rate, the mechanical build of IAR's subscription revenue and the end of a charge. The thesis begins to rely on strengths QT would have to acquire only for volume growth (United States sales execution, testing tools at 15% a year under interim leadership, renewal of IAR's converted customers) and, in the consensus version but not ours, for royalty growth of 11% a year.

The test: rebuild 2027 using only what has been demonstrated.

  • Price on developer licences of about +4.5%, realised through 2025 (the weakest year in a decade) with no rise in churn: about EUR +7 million of revenue.
  • IAR's subscription revenue recognised over the contract term from bookings already made (68% of IAR bookings were subscriptions in the second quarter of 2026 and a majority in the third): about EUR +5 million.
  • Royalties flat.
  • Costs: on my figures about EUR 13 million of the EUR 23 million of gross savings (the EUR 20 million programme plus EUR 3 million from closing IAR's security unit) were in the cost run-rate by the fourth quarter of 2026. About EUR 10 million more arrive in 2027, against about EUR 9 million of wage inflation, reinvestment and bonus normalisation.
  • The end of EUR 9.7 million of restructuring charges.

The result is revenue of about EUR 256 million (+5%), underlying EBITDA of about EUR 77 million (30%) and a rise in reported EBITA of about EUR 21 million. My base case has a rise of about EUR 26 million (from about EUR 50.6 million to EUR 76.6 million). The extra EUR 5 million needs about EUR 6.5 million of revenue from things not yet shown in the numbers: net seat growth, new customers, testing, Cyber Resilience Act conversions and royalties +2%. Even with no organic growth at all, 2027 underlying EBITDA would be about EUR 67 million, above 2026.

A second check: productivity. 2027 revenue of EUR 262 million on about 1,010 people is about EUR 260,000 a head. In 2024, the year of the 34.1% EBITA margin, it was about EUR 245,000-250,000 on about 830-860 people. The forecast asks for revenue per employee about 5% above 2024, after three years of price increases of about 5% a year, and a margin five points below 2024. It does not ask the business to be more productive than it has already been; the lower margin is IAR and three years of wage inflation.

What the precedents say (AlphaSense report on plausibility). Truecaller cut about 15-20% of staff costs in the second quarter of 2026 and was back at a 30% adjusted EBITDA margin within two quarters, with part of the saving reinvested. Yubico cut 10% of its workforce in the first quarter of 2026 and its EBIT margin went from 3% to 14.4% and 25.3% in three quarters, helped by 28% organic growth. Cerence restructured in 2024 and 2025 and did not restore its margin, because revenue fell 24% in fiscal 2025 as royalties and fixed-licence consumption fell. What separates success from failure is not the size of the cut but whether revenue holds. QT's revenue outside royalties grows on price alone, and royalties are about 20% of its revenue; its exposure to the Cerence outcome is limited to that fifth.

Where the thesis needs strengths not yet demonstrated.

  1. United States sales (about 34% of revenue). Management replaced the leadership in early 2026 and expected recovery by the end of the year; on 29 October 2026 the chief executive said he was not happy with it. The 2027 base does not need a US recovery; developer growth above 8% does.
  2. Testing at 15% a year (D-01, point 5). The EUR 100 million ambition, which DNB Carnegie dates to 2028, would need growth above 50% a year from EUR 42 million in 2026 and should be given no weight.
  3. IAR renewals. Retention was 95% under the old model (DNB Markets, April 2025) and subscribers pay 1.5-2.5 times their former payments, but the first renewals of multi-year subscriptions are still ahead, and QT's own conversion lost about 10% of customers who never converted (investor meeting, July 2025). IAR's +15% in 2027 is mostly recognition mechanics; +15% in 2028 needs renewals and new users.
  4. Forecasting of large deals. The rebuilt sales forecast has been tested only in an upswing. TeamViewer cut its revenue outlook on 3 November 2026 because large enterprise deals in Europe were delayed: the condition that broke QT's year-end in each of 2022-2025 is present among peers now.
  5. Capacity pricing and cross-selling into IAR's base. Nothing in the base case.

For the memo: 2027 is a restoration that the business has the means to deliver; it is not a stretch. The strengths still to be acquired decide whether growth after 2027 is 5% or 8%, not whether the 2027 margin step arrives.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

D504th of 6

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the cone is wide in absolute terms, narrower than QT's own record would suggest, and skewed to the downside in size but not in likelihood. For 2027 I put underlying EBITDA at EUR 82 million with an 80% range of about EUR 66-92 million, and EPS after acquired-intangible amortisation at EUR 1.92 with a range of about EUR 1.40-2.20: roughly 27% down and 15% up. The case is predictable in its costs, its prices and its direction. It is not predictable in the level of any one year's revenue until that year's fourth quarter has closed, and the fourth quarter earns about 42% of the year's EBITDA. Over 18 months that makes the forecasts moderately predictable, which for this company is an improvement.

What makes up the cone for 2027 (effects on EBITDA of EUR 82.4 million and on EPS of EUR 1.92; my arithmetic).

Source Plausible range EBITDA EPS
Organic growth 6.5%, plus or minus 4 points plus or minus about 9 plus or minus 15%
Fourth-quarter deals slipping into the next year EUR 5-8 million (2024: about EUR 8 million) -4.5 to -7 -7% to -12%; one-sided
Royalties -15% to +11% around EUR 52.5 million -7.5 to +4.5 -12% to +7%
The dollar EUR/USD 1.25 to 1.08, from about 1.155 -4 to +4 -7% to +6%
Reinvestment and other costs plus or minus EUR 3 million plus or minus 3 plus or minus 5%
Contract duration catch-up of one-year renewals, or some return to three-year terms 0 to +4 0 to +6%; one-sided

The items are not additive. Organic growth, deal timing and royalties share one cause, device makers' R&D and production budgets, so they tend to go wrong together (as in 2025); the dollar is independent. The 4-point band on organic growth is deliberately narrower than QT's record of misses against its own start-of-year guidance (about 4, 9 and 13 points in 2023, 2024 and 2025, all on the downside), because those were misses against stretch targets of 20% growth, whereas about 3 of the 6.5 points in the 2027 bar are price.

Scenarios, restated on the corrected cost base. Bear (about 20%): revenue about EUR 236 million, EBITDA EUR 60-64 million, EPS after amortisation about EUR 1.27. Base (about 60%): EUR 262 million, EUR 82.4 million, EUR 1.92. Bull (about 20%): about EUR 273 million, about EUR 93 million, about EUR 2.26. Consensus (EUR 84.6 million; EUR 1.96) sits just above the base, where the second set had it between base and bull. I put the chance that 2027 EBITDA reaches consensus at about 40-45%, and the chance that it falls more than 15% short at about 20%.

Why the skew is negative in size. Costs are fixed within the year, so a lost euro of licence revenue loses about 90 cents of EBITDA; royalties and the dollar are unhedged; and year-end slippage only runs one way within a year. On the upside, management hires into strength (incremental EBITDA margins of 36-59% in 2022-2024), so a revenue beat is partly spent. Three things make the skew less negative than the second set had it: the cost base is lower than it assumed and I have used the cautious end of my own range (fourth-quarter costs could be EUR 2-3 million lower in each year); the catch-up of one-year renewals (D-01) is upside that is not in the inputs; and the Cyber Resilience Act's December 2027 date pulls compliance purchases into 2027.

How the cone narrows over the 18 months. There are six reports, and two of them carry most of the information.

  • 18 February 2027 (fourth quarter and 2026): my ranges are revenue of EUR 72-82 million (the guidance floor implies about 72, consensus about 77, my figure 78.0) and underlying EBITDA of EUR 27-35 million. It settles the level of the cost base (the largest uncertainty in my own change to the inputs), the year's royalties and the form of 2027 guidance.
  • Late April or May 2027 (first quarter): the first quarter with IAR in both periods, and the first with the savings in full against none. After it, uncertainty about 2027 costs falls to about 2%.
  • August and late October 2027 (half year and third quarter): small quarters; they inform on royalties, the United States and IAR, not on the year's profit.
  • February 2028 (fourth quarter of 2027): about 42% of 2027 EBITDA. The cone on 2027 revenue barely narrows before this report.
  • April or May 2028 (first quarter of 2028).

Optics inside the cone. Reported revenue growth falls from +27% (third quarter of 2026) to about +1% (fourth quarter) and then runs at 6.5-8% in 2027; reported EPS roughly doubles in 2027 (about EUR 0.97 to about EUR 1.92). Both movements are mechanical (IAR entering the comparison; the restructuring charge ending) and neither is information about the business.

Against peers. Nemetschek and Planisware recognise 90-95% of revenue over the contract term and land within 1-2% of expected revenue; QT recognises 95% of a contract at signing and concentrates signings in December, so its one-year revenue error is about 4-5% and, with fixed costs, its EPS cone is three to four times as wide as that of a ratable peer with the same growth. That is a property of the accounting and the calendar, not of demand, and it will not change in the horizon.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

D503rd of 6

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: adequate, not good. For the two things that decide the next six months (the fourth-quarter cost base, and the first reading of growth without the IAR consolidation effect in the first quarter of 2027) disclosure is sufficient, because both show in lines the company publishes. For the drivers that decide whether growth after 2027 is 5% or 8% (organic ARR, IAR's revenue and renewals, testing-tool revenue, contract duration, seat counts, the amount reinvested) it publishes nothing, or a blended figure. The calendar is also thin: two results in six months, and silence between them.

What is published, and what each item tells us.

  • Revenue by type each quarter (developer licences, distribution licences since early 2026, maintenance, consulting) and by region. Royalties can be followed directly. Developer licences cannot be decomposed: the line holds price, seats, contract length, testing tools and IAR's licences together.
  • ARR each quarter since the end of 2025 (EUR 127.1 million then; EUR 164.1 million at 30 September 2026, up about 32%). Since 2026 it includes IAR, whose conversion from perpetual licences creates ARR mechanically. The organic rate (11.5% in the second quarter) is the best leading indicator in the case, and it is not reliably given. The most useful single disclosure the company could add is ARR excluding IAR.
  • Reported EBITA, restructuring costs and headcount by quarter and region. The cost programme is fully trackable (1,120 people at the end of March 2026, 1,034 at the end of June, 1,002 at the end of September; Asia-Pacific up from 169 to 203).
  • Contract assets (EUR 9.5 million at 30 September 2026 against EUR 6.4 million a year earlier), the only window on contract duration.
  • The consensus the company publishes before each report (Modular Finance, six brokers), which makes a beat or a miss on reported EBITA unambiguous.

What is not published. Organic growth; IAR's revenue, ARR and renewal rates; testing revenue; the split of one-year and three-year contracts; seats or average contract value; the pipeline; the amount of savings reinvested; the loan's margin and covenants. No quarterly guidance.

The calendar to mid-May 2027. The financial statements bulletin on 18 February 2027 (fourth quarter, 2026, guidance for 2027); the annual report on 11 March 2027 (the final purchase-price allocation for IAR and so the amortisation schedule, the loan note, personnel costs); the first-quarter statement in late April or May 2027 (date not in the files). Between reports the company says nothing unless it must. Its profit warnings for missed growth came in the first days of January (5 January 2024, 3 January 2025). Under floor-only guidance, silence in early January 2027 will mean only that the floors were met, which is not in doubt.

What will be most informative, and how to read it.

  1. The level of the 2027 floors against the informal "about 30%". In 2026 the margin floor (15%) was set about 6 points below the outcome I expect (about 21% reported). A 2027 margin floor of about 25% would be the same cushion and consistent with our base; 27% or more would show strong confidence; 22% or less would point to heavier reinvestment.
  2. Any statement of how much of the savings is reinvested. Management has given none.
  3. First-quarter 2027 growth. It will be free of the consolidation effect but not of IAR's own transition, because IAR is not split out. On my figures reported growth of 8% corresponds to about 6.5-7% excluding IAR.
  4. Distribution licences each quarter: about EUR 13 million a quarter on our forecast.

What can be followed from outside between reports. The dollar daily (the fourth-quarter 2026 average against about 1.16 a year earlier); S&P Global's monthly vehicle-production forecasts; the results of Infineon, NXP and STMicroelectronics in late January and early February, before QT reports, and of Cerence for royalties; and what TeamViewer, Nemetschek and Planisware say in February about the timing of large deals. These help with royalties and currency, the two external lines, and with the climate for year-end deals. They do not help with QT's own developer-licence signings.

Direction of travel. ARR and quarterly royalties are new in 2026, a senior investor-relations lead is being recruited, and DNB Carnegie keeps asking for segment and contract-maturity data. A split of ARR or of IAR at the February 2027 report is plausible but has not been promised.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

D503rd of 6

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the internal transmission is clear and close to linear on the cost side, and opaque and non-linear on the revenue side. The external transmission is clear for the dollar, loose for vehicle and device production, and unobservable for AI and regulation. A promise about costs can be traced through headcount and personnel expenses to EBITA and EPS within a quarter. A statement about demand cannot be traced onto a revenue line at all, because the one line that carries about 60% of revenue mixes price, seats, contract length, testing tools and IAR, and is recognised at signing.

From what the company says to EPS.

What the company says Operational driver Line it shows in Traceable?
At least EUR 20 million of annual savings, in full in 2027; EUR 3 million more from closing IAR's security unit Headcount (up to 200 roles) Personnel and other operating expenses; EBITA before restructuring Yes, each quarter
No further restructuring costs None Reported EBITA Yes
An operating margin of about 30% in 2027 Costs and revenue together EBITA margin Yes, but known only after the fourth quarter
Savings partly reinvested (Asia-Pacific sales, AI research) Hiring Headcount by region Partly; no amount is given
IAR's revenue lower in 2026, growing from 2027; a majority of bookings on subscription Conversion and renewals None: inside licence and maintenance lines No
Royalties flat to slightly lower over the medium term Customers' shipments and prepaid deals, at fixed unit prices Distribution licences Yes, each quarter
Developer-licence demand is solid; customers prefer shorter terms Price, seats, contract length Developer licences; ARR Partly; the components cannot be separated
Testing tools grow at double-digit rates; EUR 100 million ambition Testing sales None No
Capacity-based pricing over time Pricing unit None; management says do not model it No

The conversion rates are simple. One point of EBITA margin in 2027 is EUR 2.6 million, about EUR 0.08 of EPS, about 4%. EUR 1 million of cost or of royalties is about EUR 0.03 of EPS. From EBITA to EPS there are four steps: acquired-intangible amortisation (about EUR 12.5 million, schedule not disclosed until the annual report), net financial items (loan interest at an undisclosed margin, plus unhedged currency revaluation that adds noise of EUR 1-2 million a year), tax of about 20%, and 25.4 million shares. The company reports no adjusted EPS, so brokers use two bases EUR 0.39 apart and FactSet's "consensus EPS" is a blend of them. The memo should never quote an EPS figure without its basis.

The non-linear step. Under upfront recognition a EUR 1 change in a customer's annual licence value moves recognised revenue by about EUR 0.95 if the contract runs one year and by about EUR 2.60 if it runs three. The developer line therefore responds to how long customers are willing to commit as much as to how many seats they use. ARR is the linear measure of the same thing, and it is the one published in blended form (D-05).

From the value chain to EPS.

  • The dollar: clear. About 36% of revenue and a small part of costs are in dollars, with nothing hedged. One per cent on the dollar is about EUR 0.95 million of revenue and EUR 0.55 million of EBITA, about 0.9% of 2027 EPS, and it can be watched daily.
  • Customers' R&D budgets to developer seats: real, slow and not observable. Sales cycles run up to 18 months and there is no public series; the nearest evidence is what other European software vendors say about large deals.
  • Device and vehicle production to royalties: loose. The line is far more volatile than production: +49.5% in 2023, +26% in 2025, -17% in the first half of 2026 (-32% in the second quarter), +18% in the third quarter, while vehicle production moved by about 2% a year. Automotive is only 10-15% of group revenue; medical devices and consumer electronics matter as much; programme launches dominate volume; and distribution licences are sold in deals that can be large and can slip. DNB Carnegie put about EUR 8 million of fourth-quarter 2024 slippage on this line, and the fourth quarter of 2025 held large distribution deals. The line therefore behaves partly like licence deals and only partly like a metered royalty. Production data and chipmakers' guidance give its direction over a year, not its quarter. This is why the Cerence analogy applies only in part, and why I have not moved the royalty input on S&P Global's cut alone.
  • Design wins to royalties, with a lag of three to five years: named, not quantifiable. Programmes due to start or ramp in 2027-2028 include the Suzuki e Vitara, Mercedes-Benz's China-built electric E-Class (second half of 2027) and Kia's EV2, EV4 and PV5 (AlphaSense report on programmes). Unit royalties and volumes are not disclosed, so they support direction, not a number.
  • Regulation to conversions: real, unmeasured. No source gives the number of open-source users converting. About 90% of connected products fall into the Cyber Resilience Act's default class, which allows self-assessment and weakens the compulsion, and a quarter of suppliers surveyed do not know where their preparations stand (AlphaSense report on the Act). The effect will appear only as European developer-licence growth during 2027.
  • AI to seats: unobservable. It would show as smaller renewals at stable deal counts, which is not disclosed. The AlphaSense report on AI found no evidence of seat reductions in embedded tools in 2025-2026; customers are using the productivity to build more features. Elsewhere in software, pricing is moving to hybrids of seats and usage (GitHub Copilot from June 2026) and procurement teams are trimming unused seats.

What it means. The main moving parts of the 2027 forecast (costs, price, the end of a charge) are on the clear side of this divide. Those of the forecast for 2028 and after (volume, royalties, IAR renewals, AI) are on the opaque side. Predictability therefore falls with the horizon for a reason that better results alone will not cure; only disclosure will.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

B+403rd of 26

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: sentiment risk is high: QT is small, thinly covered and thinly traded, belongs to two themes the market is nervous about (AI-exposed software and automotive and industrial cyclicals), and fell about 16% in the two weeks after a strong Q3 report. A new AI-coding shock, a cluster of automotive or semiconductor warnings, or a risk-off move in small caps could take 20-30% off the shares without any change in QT's fundamentals.

The specific channels:

  1. AI de-rating. Innoscripta, with 60% margins and 43% growth, is down 55% in twelve months as an "AI loser"; QT's own multiple was pushed to cyclical lows in early 2026 by the same fear. Any major coding-agent release could restart it.
  2. Automotive and chip-cycle read-across. Infineon and STMicroelectronics fell about 40% from June to September 2026 on automotive and industrial inventory fears; carmaker profit warnings and auto-chip guidance (Infineon reported on 10 November 2026; the file predates it) move QT's royalty narrative.
  3. Risk-off and size. The Fitness for Fighting digest holds small and fragile companies at Double Underweight and momentum at Underweight; the US ten-year is above 5%; QT's market value is about EUR 0.8 billion and daily trading about USD 3.8 million, so a few sellers move the price.
  4. Coverage concentration. Four brokers in FactSet (six in the company's Modular Finance consensus), one of them commissioned research paid by the company; the buy share already fell 14 points in the last month. One downgrade matters.
  5. Opacity. Without organic growth or segment data, short sellers and sceptics can argue that IAR and contract mix hide weakness; the Risks report notes informal short campaigns exploit this.

Mitigants: short interest is below reporting thresholds, there has never been a formal short report, and the Ingman group's 21.6% is a stable anchor.

Track record (guidance raise/sell-side upgrades) ~L2-3Y

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?D6015th of 26full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

D4812th of 26

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: the record of upgrading is poor: in the last three years guidance was narrowed down (2024) or cut (2025, twice) and never raised; in 2026 it has not been raised despite large headroom. The behaviour has shifted from optimistic ranges that had to be cut to conservative floors that are not raised. That shift is good for credibility but means guidance carries no information about the upside.

History: 2024 guidance of 20-30% growth was narrowed to 20-25% and the company delivered 15.7% (a 4.3-point miss against the narrowed range); 2025 guidance of 15-25% at constant currency was cut to 3-10% after deals slipped in Q3 and a profit warning on 17 October 2025, and delivered 6.6% at constant currency; 2022 initial targets were also missed. 2026: floors set in February and reiterated three times, with nine-month growth about twice the revenue floor.

The chief executive's behaviour: candid about failures (he admitted the prior-year guidance was poor), now explicitly refusing to give an upper bound. The Wisdom Library's "sandbagging" archetype describes management teams that set beatable targets and then hit them precisely; QT has adopted the policy but has only one year of evidence. The risk is the reverse of the old one: the market may treat every year as a beat-and-hold, and a year in which the floor is merely met would read as a disappointment.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

D5015th of 26

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: mixed, and strongly dependent on how high the bar was set. Over the last year the company has beaten consensus on revenue in three of four quarters (Q4 2025, Q2 2026, Q3 2026) against low expectations; over three years it missed guidance in 2024 and 2025 and cut guidance twice in 2025; over five years it also missed in 2022. In comparable conditions (a cyclical trough with low expectations and a cost reset) it has beaten; in conditions where it set the bar (2022, 2024, 2025) it has missed.

One year:

  • Q4 2025: revenue EUR 77.1 million, 4% above Inderes; adjusted EBITA EUR 27.4 million, 25% above consensus.
  • Q1 2026: weak (EBITA margin 9.6%), and February's 2026 guidance was 43% below consensus.
  • Q2 2026: revenue EUR 61.3 million against EUR 56.7 million (+8.1%); EBITA EUR 9.3 million against 6.2 million (+50%).
  • Q3 2026: revenue EUR 51.7 million against 48.3 million (+7.1%); reported EBITA EUR 5.7 million against 6.2 million (a miss caused by EUR 5.3 million of restructuring; underlying EUR 11.0 million was well above); EPS EUR 0.04 against 0.05.

Three years: 2024 guidance narrowed from 20-30% to 20-25%, delivered 15.7% (deals slipped at year-end); 2025 guidance of 15-25% cut to 3-10% after three quarters of missed internal forecasts, with a profit warning on 17 October 2025; sell-side estimates were cut repeatedly and lagged the business on the way down and on the way up.

Five years: initial 2022 targets also missed late in the year. The pattern: misses come from large deals slipping across quarter-ends in slowing markets and from planning on optimistic growth; beats come when the bar is low.

For the model: weight management's floors as near-certain and its growth ambitions with a discount; expect Q4 to carry most of the risk in any year.

P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

C+556th of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: QT's record splits cleanly by type of promise, and the forecasts should be weighted the same way. Commitments about costs, integration and conversion have been met or beaten (high weight); growth plans have been missed in three of the last four years (heavy discount); and every miss has come from large deals slipping in a Q3 or Q4 of a slowing market. So: the 2026 forecast deserves high weight and a lean slightly above consensus on margin; the 2027 margin deserves moderately high weight because about two thirds of the step-up is already-executed self-help, but should be leaned slightly below management's "about 30%" and about 7% below consensus EBITDA; 2027 revenue deserves moderate weight and a lean slightly below consensus (royalties); and 2028-2030 deserve low weight, leaned below consensus on margin and below management's long-term ambitions on growth.

The record, by type of promise.

Promise What was promised What was delivered Verdict

Growth guidance 2026 at least 10% at constant currency; EBITA margin at least 15% on track for about 14% at constant currency and about 18.7% reported EBITA (my estimate) Floors set to be beaten
