The beats are all in a period of low expectations and cost-led margins. The sell-side has lagged in both directions: 2026 EPS fell from EUR 3.22 to EUR 0.905 before recovering to EUR 1.17, and 2027 revenue estimates are still 9% below where they were twelve months ago while 2027 EPS estimates are up 46% in six months, so the recovery in forecasts is cost-led, not demand-led.

Why the record splits this way. The chief executive is a capital-markets strategist who sets bold growth plans and then acts decisively on costs when they fail; sales forecasting was "broken" in 2025 by his own account and was rebuilt, but has been tested only in a recovery. The company now publishes floors only, which removes the risk of a guidance cut but also removes the information: a floor that is merely met would now read as a disappointment.

How much weight, year by year.

  • Q4 2026 and full-year 2026 (high weight; my error band about ±3% on revenue, ±8% on EBITDA). Three quarters are reported; the cost base is visible; the floors need only EUR 72 million of Q4 revenue and about EUR 16 million of Q4 reported EBITA. Lean: revenue in line to slightly above consensus (my EUR 243.7 million against EUR 243 million), underlying EBITDA above (EUR 61.5 million against EUR 58.1 million) because consensus implies Q4 costs rising faster than the run-rate. The risk to this lean is the company's own history of Q4 deal slippage; Q4 2025 also included large distribution licence deals that may not repeat.
  • 2027 margin (moderately high weight; ±12% on EBITDA). About EUR 18-19 million of the EUR 27.5 million rise in reported EBITA is the end of restructuring charges and net savings already executed. Lean: EBITA margin 27.8%, below management's informal "about 30%", because management itself has said savings will partly be reinvested and its growth plans have been optimistic; consensus EBITDA (EUR 84.6 million) needs costs to fall in 2027, mine rise about 1%.
  • 2027 revenue (moderate weight; ±5%). Lean: EUR 262 million against consensus EUR 264 million; developer licences in line, royalties about EUR 5 million below consensus, IAR about EUR 1 million below.
  • 2028-2030 (low weight; ±15-20% on EBITDA by 2029). Lean: margins below consensus (my incremental EBITDA margin 55% against consensus 67% in 2028, history 49-59%), growth of 7-8% against management's long-term organic aim of about 10% and below the pace needed for the EUR 100 million testing ambition (my testing revenue is about EUR 70 million in 2030).

Direction of lean against each external reference (for the fourth set). Against company guidance: above (2026 floors). Against management's informal 2027 margin: slightly below. Against 2027 consensus: in line on revenue, about 7% below on EBITDA and about 8% below on EPS on the brokers' after-amortisation basis (my EUR 1.80 against EUR 1.96; on Inderes' basis EUR 2.20 against EUR 2.47). Against 2028 consensus: in line on revenue, about 8% below on EBITDA, about 6% below on EPS (my EUR 2.28 against EUR 2.42 after amortisation), with the gap narrowed by lower interest.

The critical caution for the third set. My lean below consensus in 2027 is a lean on judgement (reinvestment, royalties), not on mechanics; the mechanics (savings, restructuring end, IAR in both periods) favour consensus. If Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 margins land where I expect (35.5% and about 22%), the 2027 debate narrows to reinvestment and royalties, both of which management will describe at the February 2027 report.

Forecasts with good growth rates and higher growth/margins than past

Growth rate

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

A612nd of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts describe a high-confidence, cost-led earnings recovery in 2027 followed by a lower-confidence, demand-led compounding phase, and the investment-relevant point is that these two phases have very different predictability. For 2026 and 2027 the largest drivers are already in the company's hands: EUR 23 million of gross savings (about EUR 16-18 million net of reinvestment), the end of EUR 10.5 million of restructuring charges, IAR's return to growth once its subscription base builds, and debt falling towards zero; about two thirds of the EUR 27.5 million rise in reported EBITA from 2026 to 2027 is self-help already executed. That makes a 2027 EBITDA margin of about 30% (EBITA about 28%) more predictable than anything QT has forecast for itself since 2021, and it is why I would weight the 2027 margin highly despite the company's poor record on growth plans. The judgement calls sit in a narrow band: whether reinvestment is about EUR 5 million (my view, and management's own caution) or close to zero (consensus), and whether royalties grow about 2% (my view, and management's "flat to slightly lower") or 11% (consensus). Each is worth about 2 points of 2027 margin and together they explain my 7-8% shortfall against 2027-2028 consensus EBITDA and EPS. Beyond 2027 the forecast rests on diffuse, external drivers (organic developer growth, royalty volumes, IAR renewals, the dollar) and on loops that move slowly and roughly cancel, so the cone widens even as the reported numbers become cleaner. Three things the third and fourth sets should carry forward: the bear case is further from the base than the bull, because costs are fixed within the year and currency and royalties are unhedged, while upside is partly reinvested; headline growth will fall from +27% to about +1% and then +8% for mechanical reasons around the February 2027 report, and reported EPS will jump about 120% in 2027 for equally mechanical reasons; and from 2028 the balance sheet turns into net cash that, on the chief executive's record, is more likely to fund acquisitions than buybacks, which would change growth, amortisation and predictability in ways no broker models. Net, the forecasts are positive for the case: earnings roughly double from the 2026 trough by 2028 on assumptions that need no heroics, but they support a return to the 2023-2024 economics on lower growth rather than a new, higher earnings path.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

A641st of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: 2026 carries the heaviest below-operating drag in the company's history (acquired-intangible amortisation, restructuring and financial costs together of about EUR 29 million pre-tax, about EUR 0.91 per share), and from 2027 that drag unwinds fast: restructuring stops, interest falls to near zero by 2028 and turns to income in 2029, and the tax rate eases with Finland's planned rate cut. Below-operating items alone add about EUR 0.58 to reported EPS between 2026 and 2028, about 40% of the rise from EUR 0.82 to EUR 2.28. There are no minorities or associates, no dividend and no buyback in the base case, and the share count is effectively flat. Free cash flow converts at about 0.9-1.0 times adjusted net income and 1.05-1.10 times reported net income from 2027, because acquired-intangible amortisation is not cash.

Each item, with my inputs.

  • Amortisation of acquired intangibles (excluded from adjusted EPS and from the model's D&A). EUR 3.13 million in Q3 2026, about EUR 12.5 million a year; the IAR purchase-price allocation recognised EUR 114.2 million of intangibles (technology EUR 45.4 million, customer relationships EUR 38.7 million, trademarks EUR 5.8 million). No schedule is disclosed. I assume EUR 12.5 million in 2026-2027, then 12.0 / 11.0 / 10.0 as the older froglogic (2021) and Axivion (2022) technology assets complete their lives; that is my assumption. Effect: EUR 0.39 per share in 2027, falling to about EUR 0.31 by 2030. It is the whole of the gap between Inderes' EPS and the other three brokers' EPS.
  • Restructuring and other one-offs. EUR 9.7 million in January-September 2026; I add EUR 0.8 million in Q4 for the European countries finishing late in 2026 (DNB Carnegie warned of spill-over integration costs), so EUR 10.5 million for the year and nothing after. Cash: about EUR 7.5 million paid in 2026, EUR 3 million in early 2027.
  • Net financial items. EUR -6.3 million (2026), -3.1, -0.5, +1.2, +2.5 (P-02). Foreign-exchange revaluation of foreign-currency receivables (EUR 32.1 million at end-2025) and intercompany balances adds noise of about ±EUR 1-2 million a year in either direction; it was a EUR 1.8 million loss in 2025 and a loss again in H1 2026.
  • Tax. 21.0% in 2025 (19% in 2024, 18.5% reported), raised by non-deductible share-based payments and foreign rates. Finland plans to cut the corporate rate from 20% to 18% from 2027 (AlphaSense EPS report, citing the policy; Fortum expects about 1 point off its group rate); Sweden is 20.6%, the US 21% plus state taxes. My rates: 21.5% (2026, restructuring partly non-deductible), 20.0% (2027), 19.5% (2028-2030). If the Finnish cut were not enacted, add about 0.7 points from 2027 (about 1% of EPS).
  • Share count. 25.4 million. The 2025-2027 performance share plan (maximum 214,000 shares, 0.8%) requires 2027 revenue above EUR 333 million and will almost certainly pay nothing; new plans and option exercises historically added 0.1-0.5 million shares a year (24.8 million in 2021 to 25.4 million in 2023). I add 0.05 million a year from 2027.
  • Buybacks, issuance, dividends. None in the base case (no dividend since the 2016 demerger; buyback authorisation for 2 million shares unused; EUR 11 million of treasury shares bought in 2022 only to manage the share count). The option is real from 2028 (P-02).
  • Minorities, associates, pensions. None (FactSet capital structure: associates, pensions and minorities all zero).

The bridge in reported EPS from 2026 to 2028 (my figures). EUR 0.82 to EUR 2.28 (+EUR 1.46). Below-operating items contribute about +EUR 0.58: the end of restructuring +0.32, lower financial costs +0.18, the lower tax rate +0.06, slightly lower amortisation +0.02. Operating improvement contributes the remaining about +EUR 0.88. On the adjusted basis (EUR 1.54 to EUR 2.66), only the financial items and tax help (about +EUR 0.24); the rest is operating.

Against the last four to six years. Between 2020 and 2024 the below-operating items were almost invisible: net interest of EUR -0.1 to -2.0 million, tax of 19-23% (8.9% in 2022 was a one-off), amortisation of acquired intangibles of about EUR 6-7 million after the froglogic and Axivion deals, and no restructuring. 2024 was flattered by a EUR 6.7 million earn-out revaluation gain in financial income (about EUR 0.25 per share, so underlying 2024 EPS was about EUR 2.0, not EUR 2.26). 2026 is therefore the outlier; by 2028 the below-operating drag is back to about EUR 12.5 million pre-tax (almost all amortisation, about EUR 0.38 per share), and by 2030 it is about EUR 7.5 million net (amortisation less interest income). The EPS trajectory from 2026 to 2028 looks steeper than the business improvement because of this normalisation, which the narrative may read as stronger operating momentum than it is.

Where I differ from the first set. The first set put 2026 net interest at EUR 4-5 million; foreign-exchange losses push it to about EUR 6 million. And its estimate that falling interest adds "EUR 0.10-0.15 to EPS by 2028" is right in total (my +EUR 0.18 including the 2026 foreign-exchange losses that do not recur).

7b

Margins/growth vs. past

6 answers
C-5360C-7864C-8038C-6755P-0465P-0961
C-53How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?C-6011th of 26full answer under 4c Financial profile
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

B645th of 26

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: the scope is substantial and concentrated in four big areas: the end of IAR's subscription trough and the uplift from subscription pricing, a recovery in organic developer licence growth (Europe, Asia and a fixed US), testing growth towards EUR 100 million, and a move to capacity-based pricing. Peer and own-history precedents show margins of 30-38% are achievable; all four areas are the stated strategy.

  1. IAR. From about EUR 33 million of revenue in 2026 to about EUR 47 million in 2028 on segment consensus, back to profit; industry precedents show subscription conversion raises lifetime customer value 30-40% (ATOSS, Temenos, per the Industry digest). IAR earned a 38% EBITDA margin before the transition.
  2. Organic developer licences. Price increases of about 5% a year plus a seat recovery as Western R&D restarts; the 1-year contract cycle now compounds price faster. A fixed US sales team is the swing (about a third of revenue).
  3. Testing. A 20% growth rate takes the unit from about EUR 40 million (2026) to about EUR 70 million by 2029, which adds about EUR 25-30 million of high-margin revenue.
  4. Pricing model. Capacity and usage pricing for testing and compliance, and possibly for development, would decouple revenue from seat counts. Not yet in any forecast.

Smaller areas: CRA-driven conversions of open-source users, royalties from the 2022-2025 design wins, working-capital release, and lower interest.

Precedents: QT itself reached a 34.1% EBITA margin in 2024; IAR 38% EBITDA before the transition; Planisware 37% EBITDA with 13-14% growth; peers' EBIT margins about 30%. The weaker precedent: QT's own 2025 shows how quickly the margin falls when growth is planned too high.

For the model, this makes an EBITA margin of 32-35% plausible by 2028-2029 if organic growth reaches 8-10%; at 4-5% organic growth, the margin would stall at about 27-29%.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

F3824th of 26

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: yes, severely. Reported growth over the last twelve months (+17%) hides an organic engine running at roughly 3-5%, about a quarter of the company's normal rate (16-18% at constant currency in 2023-2024; about 29% a year in 2019-2024). The input force (customers' new product programmes) has stopped slowing and is recovering in Europe and Asia, but not yet in the US or in Western automotive, and royalties are still falling.

The arithmetic: revenue for Q4 2025 to Q3 2026 was EUR 242.8 million against EUR 207.6 million a year earlier (+17%). IAR contributes about EUR 30 million of the EUR 35 million increase (about EUR 8 million a quarter), and currency was a net drag of about EUR 3 million. That leaves organic constant-currency growth of roughly 3-5%, consistent with the company's 2.6% for 2025, DNB Carnegie's about 3% for Q2 2026 and its 2.9% estimate for 2026.

By engine:

  • Developer licences: stabilised and growing again in Europe (Q3 2026), with Asia solid and the US weak; customers buy fewer seats and shorter terms. Organic ARR, excluding IAR, grew 11.5% in Q2 2026, which shows the recurring base still compounding (price increases plus renewals) faster than recognised revenue.
  • Distribution royalties: -17.4% in H1 2026, +18.2% in Q3 (a timing effect, per management); consensus -9% for 2026; management expects flat to slightly lower over the medium term. This engine is still decelerating relative to 2025's +26%.
  • Testing: growing faster than the group, but not disclosed.

The input force: Western carmakers are still cutting R&D and light-vehicle production is falling (Q3 2026 -2%, Q4 expected -4%); Chinese carmakers, defence and medical are investing. The force has turned from contraction to mixed, not to expansion.

For the model: the organic engine should be modelled at 2-4% for 2026 and 6-9% for 2027, with the upside case requiring a Western automotive restart; the risk in reading the reported +19-27% growth as a recovery is exactly the "false friend" the Risks report warns of.

C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

C558th of 26

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: consensus forecasts a return to the 2023-2024 margin structure on growth well below the 2019-2024 rate: revenue +12.4%, +8.5% and +7.8% in 2026-2028 (against about 29% a year in 2019-2024 and 3.5% in 2025), EBITDA margins back to 32-35% and EPS of EUR 1.96 in 2027 and 2.42 in 2028. The revenue path is credible; the 2027 margin is at the upper end of what the building blocks support; the royalty line looks too high against management's own words. Overall, 2027 consensus EPS carries a modest downside skew of about 5-10%, and 2028 depends on royalties and organic growth.

Line by line (FactSet median; history from the block):

  • Revenue: 243 / 264 / 284 / 299. 2026 needs Q4 of about EUR 77 million, flat on Q4 2025 (credible). 2027's +8.5% is about 2.7 points from IAR (EUR 33 million to 39.6 million) and about 6 points organic (credible if Europe holds and the US stabilises). Brokers range from EUR 261 million (Inderes) to 287 million (DNB Carnegie, which assumes 15% organic growth): a 10% spread.
  • Segments (FactSet consensus): developer licences 159 / 172 / 186 (+8% a year, plausible on price plus modest seat growth); distribution licences 51.9 / 57.8 / 64.2 (-9%, then +11% a year), which conflicts with management's "flat to slightly down over the medium term" and is the line most likely to be cut; maintenance about 29-30 flat; IAR 33.0 / 39.6 / 47.4 (+20% a year, plausible as subscriptions build).
  • Margins: EBITDA margin (brokers' basis) 23.9% / 32.1% / 34.5% / 35.8% against 32.2% (2023) and 35.7% (2024) on FactSet's operating basis; EBIT margin 16% / 25% / 28%. On our cost bridge (C-75), a 30% EBITA margin in 2027 needs nearly all of the EUR 20 million of savings to be incremental to 2026 and little reinvestment, or organic growth above 8%; our centre is about 28-29%.
  • Below EBIT: financial costs of about EUR 4-5 million a year look high as debt falls from EUR 120 million to near zero (C-76); tax about 20% (in line with history); share count flat (correct: no buybacks).
  • Cash: free cash flow 40.8 / 53.8 / 62.8; capex 1.9-2.4 (about 1% of revenue, in line); total debt 80 / 40 / 1.
  • A reconciliation trap: consensus D&A of EUR 5.6-6.0 million does not reconcile with EBITDA less EBIT (about EUR 19 million in 2026 and 18 million in 2027); the difference is acquired-intangible amortisation (about EUR 12.5 million) and lease depreciation. Model these explicitly.

Credibility given the foundations: the foundations (pricing power, high incremental margins, near-zero capex) make the margin restoration plausible; the change forces (weak Western automotive, royalties flat, partial reinvestment of savings) make the 2027 timing and the royalty growth the weak points.

P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

B+613rd of 18

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the net result is the same margin machine running on about a third of its former growth rate, with the cash it used to spend on hiring and acquisitions piling up. Against 2019-2024, revenue growth falls from about 29% a year (22% in 2020-2025) to about 7-8% (organic about 6.5-7%); margins return to, but only at the end of the horizon regain, the 2024 peak; EPS growth after the 2027 rebound settles at 12-21% a year; and free cash flow roughly doubles from the 2023-2025 average. Against the 2025-2026 trough the impact is strongly positive and, for 2027, unusually predictable for this company because it is mostly self-help. Two optical effects will make the reported figures misleading in opposite directions over the next 18 months: headline revenue growth collapses from +27% (Q3 2026) to about +1% (Q4 2026) and +8% (Q1 2027) for purely mechanical reasons, while headline reported EPS growth of about +120% in 2027 overstates the operating improvement.

History against forecast.

Line 2019-2024 2025 2026 (mine) 2027-2030 (mine)
Revenue growth about 29% a year (19% in 2023-2024) +3.5% (organic +2.6%) +12.7% (organic about +4%) 7.0-8.0% a year (organic 6.5-7%)
EBITDA margin (FactSet operating; mine underlying) 3.7% to 35.7%; 2021-2024 average 31% 25.6% 25.2% 30.0%, 32.0%, 33.5%, 34.5%
Incremental EBITDA margin 36-59% (2022-2024) negative about 20% (savings offset by IAR's full-year costs) 93% in 2027 (self-help), 55% from 2028
EPS (reported) EUR 0.53 (2020) to 2.26 (2024; about 2.0 underlying) 1.25 0.82 1.80, 2.28, 2.71, 3.09
Free cash flow (FactSet definition, before leases) EUR 39-52 million in 2023-2024 39.1 about 34.5 about 53, 67, 78, 85

Return on capital employed (pre-tax, incl. goodwill) 43.4% (2024) 15.5% about 18% about 24% (2027), above 30% by 2030 on capital employed net of the cash build

What drives the difference from history, and how concentrated it is. For 2027 against 2026, the drivers are concentrated and mostly controllable: the end of restructuring charges, EUR 7.5-8.5 million of net incremental savings and IAR's return to growth explain most of the EBITA rise, and organic revenue growth of about 6.5% explains the rest. For 2028-2030, the drivers become diffuse and external: organic developer growth (price about 4.5 points a year plus volume), royalty volumes, IAR renewals, testing growth and the dollar. The forecast therefore becomes less predictable after 2027 at the same time as the reported figures become cleaner.

Optical effects the third and fourth sets must not misread.

  1. The headline growth cliff. Reported growth was +11.6%, +19.6% and +27.0% in Q1-Q3 2026, flattered by IAR's first-year consolidation. Q4 2026 compares with a Q4 2025 that already contained 2.5 months of IAR (EUR 8.1 million) and large distribution licence deals: my +1.2% (EUR 78.0 million) is consistent with organic growth of about +2%. From Q1 2027 IAR is in both periods and reported growth is close to organic plus IAR's own recovery: about +6.5-8% a quarter in 2027. The market has been paying for the headline; the narrative risk is that a healthy organic business shows a sharp "deceleration" at the 18 February 2027 report.
  2. The reported EPS jump. Reported EPS rises about 120% in 2027 (EUR 0.82 to EUR 1.80) because EUR 10.5 million of restructuring disappears; adjusted EPS rises 43%; underlying EBITDA rises 28%.
  3. Cash turning to net cash. From mid-2028 the balance sheet carries growing idle cash that earns little; on my base case it reaches about EUR 200 million (about a quarter of today's market value) by end-2030 unless it is spent.

The net judgement for the investment case. The forecasts imply a business returning to its 2023-2024 profitability and cash generation, but on structurally lower growth; at about 13.4 times 24-month-forward earnings (the 7th percentile of five years), the market prices something close to my base case rather than the consensus path, so the forecast is supportive but not by itself a re-rating case. The re-rating case needs organic growth near 10% (which would add about EUR 0.20-0.25 to 2028 EPS against mine) or a capital-allocation decision on the cash.

The fourth set as a whole

C+542nd of 6

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Judgement: for the next six months, little of our assessment is missing from guidance, from estimates or from the brokers' narrative, and the gap the earlier sets thought they had found (a fourth-quarter beat, followed by 2027 estimates that were too high) is not there on the corrected numbers. Guidance is a floor that will be beaten and tells the market nothing new. Consensus for 2027 is within 3% of our base case. The brokers describe the same recovery that we do. What is not reflected lies on the market's side, not the sell-side's: the shares trade on the sector-median multiple with no premium, while our work finds the 2027 recovery well founded, with about 80% of it resting on strengths already shown. The favourable gap is one of confidence and of multiple, not of estimates. It closes through delivery over several quarters, not through an event, and the first report that can begin to close it is the first quarter of 2027.

By horizon.

  • The next six months. On 18 February 2027: floors beaten, as expected; a fourth quarter in line with consensus; 2027 floors below consensus by design; royalty estimates trimmed; headline growth of about +1%; revision momentum fading by arithmetic. I see no positive catalyst for estimates in that report and some risk of a flat or soft reception, as in October. In late April or May 2027: growth excluding IAR, the first figure that can change the narrative, with about a 45% chance of being good enough to start demand-led upgrades.
  • Months 6 to 18. The 2027 margin step arrives quarter by quarter and reported EPS roughly doubles; both are expected. Net debt approaches zero around the end of 2027; the Cyber Resilience Act applies in full from December 2027; new mid-term targets are possible. The fourth quarter of 2027, reported in February 2028, is the largest single test.
  • 18 months to three years. Consensus 2028 EBITDA is more likely to be trimmed than raised (royalties and the incremental margin), with EPS supported by lower financial costs. The things that could change the picture (the use of net cash of about EUR 55 million at the end of 2028 and about EUR 130 million at the end of 2029 on the revised base case, capacity pricing, new disclosure) are in no forecast and in no narrative.

My steer to the memo.

  1. On guidance and conservatism: guidance is conservative in form and uninformative in content. Management does keep expectations low, but the record of raising guidance is nil and the new policy will not produce raises. Do not present guidance as a source of upgrades.
  2. On scope for upgrades over 6-12 months: modest, and conditional on organic growth. The cost-led upgrades are done. The identifiable positives are small (financial costs; the low end of my cost range; the renewal catch-up), and one identifiable negative, royalties, is of a similar size.
  3. On momentum: current readings are good (technical momentum A, sell-side momentum B), but the sell-side reading will fade as the May-to-August 2026 upgrades leave the six-month window.
  4. On where the stock is in its cycle: turning up, and past the bottom. Estimates troughed in May 2026 and the price at about EUR 18 earlier in 2026. It is not yet in a phase in which the thesis is broadening: revisions are cost-led and the multiple has not moved. The next phase needs revenue-led revisions.
  5. On the multiple and the return: see D-07. The return shown at the guideline multiple of 43x should not be presented as what the evidence supports; a range of about 16x to 24x on the consensus basis is what I can defend, the lower end if the case stays cost-led.
  6. What favours the case: expectations in the price are low; the 2027 forecast is well founded and not above what the business has done before; consensus for 2027 is not stretched; the downside in the next quarter is cushioned by floors that are certain to be met. What does not: we have no numerical edge on the next six to twelve months; the next report is unlikely to be a catalyst; and the two lines on which consensus looks too high are for 2028.
  7. What would change this judgement: if the brokers' 2026 figures prove to be before restructuring (about 20%), the fourth quarter becomes a clear beat and the score for the estimates gap rises by about ten points. If the 2027 margin floor is set at 22% or less, or royalties in the fourth quarter fall well short of about EUR 13 million, it falls.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

A571st of 6

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: the 2026 floors will be beaten on 18 February 2027 (about 97%), and since consensus already sits far above them this carries no surprise. Guidance for 2027 will most likely be floors again (about 70%), set below consensus by design; I expect something like growth of at least 5% at constant currency and an EBITA margin of at least 25%, against consensus of about 8.5% and 30%. Once set, the floors are much more likely to be met and left unchanged (about 80%) than raised (about 10%) or cut (about 10%). Formal guidance therefore tells the market little. The guidance that matters is informal: whether management repeats "about 30%" for 2027 in February, and what it says about reinvestment. Further out the stated ambitions divide. A margin of about 30% or more is likely to be met and could be made formal; organic growth of about 10% and testing revenue of EUR 100 million are more likely to be undershot than met.

Event by event.

Event Guidance in play Raised Met or beaten, left unchanged Cut or missed
18 February 2027 2026 floors: growth of at least 10% at constant currency; EBITA margin of at least 15% Not applicable; a raise before the report is under 5% About 97% beaten About 3%
18 February 2027 New guidance for 2027 Form: floors about 70%; floors with a stated margin level or a range about 25% (Inderes expects margin targets); something else about 5%
Late April or May, August, late October 2027 2027 floors About 10% across the three About 80% About 10%
February 2028 2027 outcome against the floors; new guidance for 2028 About 85-90% beaten About 10-15%
April or May 2028 2028 floors Low High Low

The 2026 floors. They need fourth-quarter revenue of about EUR 72 million and fourth-quarter reported EBITA of about EUR 16-17 million. My figures are EUR 78.0 million and about EUR 30.6 million; consensus implies about EUR 77 million and about EUR 32 million. Only a year-end collapse in signings of the kind seen in no year, including 2025, would breach them. The floors were not raised in May, August or October despite nine-month growth of about 21% at constant currency, and they will not be raised now.

The 2027 guidance: its form and level. Management adopted floors "to avoid consecutive downgrades" after cutting guidance twice in 2025, and the policy has worked for it: no warning, three reiterations, credibility rebuilt. I see no reason for it to change after one year. For the level, the 2026 precedent is a margin floor about 6 points below the outcome I expect (15% against about 21% reported) and a growth floor about 4 points below (10% against about 14%). Applied to what management appears to expect for 2027 (a margin of about 30%; growth in the high single digits now that IAR is in both periods), that gives a margin floor of about 25% (a range of 22-27%) and a growth floor of about 5% (3-7%). In February 2026 the first floors were about 43% below consensus profit and were received as a disappointment. A 25% floor would be about 17% below consensus EBITA, and the policy is now understood, so I expect a muted response. The level still carries information: 27% or more would show confidence; 22% or less, or the disappearance of the "about 30%" language, would be read as reinvestment absorbing the savings and would take 3-5% off 2027 estimates.

Why a raise during 2027 is unlikely. The policy is not to raise, and there is a structural reason behind it: the fourth quarter earns about 42% of the year's EBITDA and its largest contracts are signed in the last days of December, so management does not itself know the year until it is over. A raise would be a refinement of wording at most.

Why a cut is unlikely but not negligible. A cut needs the outcome to fall through a floor set about 5 points of margin and 2-3 points of growth below expectation. My bear case (about 20% likely: revenue of about EUR 236 million, an EBITA margin of about 23-24%) would breach floors of 5% and 25%; some of that case is currency, which hurts the reported margin but not constant-currency growth. That gives about 10-15% if the floors are set where I expect, and 5-8% if they are set lower. The timing would follow the pattern of 2025: a warning with the third-quarter report in October 2027, or in the first days of January 2028.

The informal guidance. I put the chance that the chief executive repeats an operating margin of "about 30%" for 2027 in February at about 60%, softens it ("towards 30%", "the high twenties") at about 30%, and raises it at about 10%. On my corrected figures the 2027 EBITA margin is 29.2%, so "about 30%" is a fair description of the base case; on the earlier sets' 27.8% it was not. What can defeat it is the fourth quarter of 2027, and that will not be known until February 2028.

Secondarily: targets and ambitions for 18 months to three years out.

  • The board's 2023 target of 20-30% annual growth is dead in practice. I expect it to be replaced formally during 2027, at the February report or a capital markets day (about 60%). That is a reduction, and one the market already assumes.
  • A structural operating margin of about 30% from 2027. My figures are 31.1% for 2028 and 32.6% for 2029. Likely to be met (about 65-70%), and it could be raised or widened to "above 30%" when new targets are set (about 25%).
  • Long-term organic growth of about 10%. My figures are 6.5-7%. It has no date, so it will not be "cut"; it is more likely to be undershot in 2028 and 2029 than met (met: about 25%).
  • Testing revenue of EUR 100 million. The company gives no date; DNB Carnegie's 2028 is out of reach (my figures: about EUR 53 million in 2028 and EUR 61 million in 2029).
  • IAR: a majority of customers on subscription within three years of the acquisition. On track (68% of bookings in the second quarter of 2026); likely met.
  • The 2025-2027 share plan (2027 revenue above EUR 333 million) will pay nothing. A replacement plan is likely in 2027, and its thresholds will be the best evidence of what the board itself expects for 2028-2029. It is worth reading when it is published.

C+502nd of 6

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: the cost-led upgrade cycle that lifted consensus 2027 EPS by 46% in six months is largely complete, and consensus for 2027 and 2028 now sits about where I do. Over the next few events I expect consensus EPS to be held within about 5% of today's level, with a change of composition underneath (royalties cut; costs and financial items improved), and I expect the net breadth of revisions to fall from strongly positive to roughly balanced. The next sustained move in estimates needs revenue to be revised, which needs evidence of organic growth, and the first date on which that can arrive is the first-quarter report in late April or May 2027. For 2028 and beyond, the more likely direction is a modest cut to EBITDA (3-6%) with EPS held, because consensus carries royalty growth of 11% a year and an incremental margin of 67% but also financial costs that are too high.

Where consensus stands against my base case, like for like (EUR million; EPS in EUR).

Three features of this consensus matter for how it will move:

  1. It is four brokers, and one is an outlier. DNB Carnegie's estimates in FactSet are dated 6 August 2026 and have 2027 revenue of EUR 287 million (growth of about 15%) and 2028 revenue of EUR 328 million and EBITDA of EUR 111 million, 9-16% above the other three. When they are refreshed the mean will fall; the median, which is OP's figure, will not. Quote the median.
  2. Its EPS is a blend of bases. Inderes (EUR 2.47 for 2027) is before acquired-intangible amortisation; the other three (EUR 1.81-1.99) are after it. The mean of the three is EUR 1.92, the same as mine.
  3. Its recovery has come from costs. Over six months 2027 EPS is up 46% and EBITDA 28%, but sales only 5%; over twelve months sales estimates are still 9% lower. The share of buy ratings is 29%, down 14 points in a month, and the mean target of EUR 37.9 is about 24% above the price.

Event by event.

  • 18 February 2027. On my figures the quarter is in line: revenue of EUR 78.0 million against about EUR 77 million, reported EBITA of about EUR 30.6 million against about EUR 32 million. I do not expect a repeat of the 7-8% revenue beats of the second and third quarters. Revisions to 2027 will then turn on three things. Royalties: the 2026 outcome (about EUR 51.5 million) will be known, management's "flat to slightly lower" will probably be repeated, and brokers carrying +11% are likely to trim 2027 by EUR 2-5 million, worth 2-6% of EBITDA. Costs: the fourth-quarter cost base and whatever is said about reinvestment; consensus 2027 costs (about EUR 179 million) are close to mine (about EUR 180 million), so a reinvestment figure of about EUR 5 million changes nothing and a larger one cuts. Financial items: lower interest as the loan amortises. My distribution for the change in consensus 2027 EPS after this report: up more than 2%, about 25%; within 2% either way, about 45%; down more than 2%, about 30%.
  • The exception. If my reading of the consensus basis is wrong (about 20%), the fourth quarter would beat consensus EBITDA by about EUR 8 million and 2026-2027 estimates would rise by 5-10%. This is the main upside risk to a call of "held".
  • Late April or May 2027. The first quarter with IAR in both periods. My figures are revenue growth of 8% and an underlying EBITDA margin of 22.2% against 12.2%. If growth excluding IAR is 6-7% or better (about 45%), revenue estimates, which have barely moved in six months, can rise, and these would be the first demand-led upgrades of the cycle. If it is 4% or less (about 25%), revenue estimates fall by 2-3% and EPS by 6-9%.
  • August and late October 2027. Small quarters; estimates drift with royalties and the dollar. October is when a problem with year-end deals would first be visible, as in 2025.
  • February 2028. The fourth quarter of 2027 decides the year and sets the base for 2028.

Net breadth. With four brokers, breadth is lumpy and can swing on one analyst. I expect it to be roughly balanced through February 2027; its direction after April depends on organic growth.

A mechanical point about revision momentum. The six-month revision of +46% is measured from the trough of May 2026 (EUR 1.36). By February 2027 the six-month window will begin after the August 2026 upgrades and will show about +8% even if no estimate changes; by May 2027 it will show about zero. The dashboard's sell-side momentum rating of B (score 11) will therefore fade by arithmetic alone unless new upgrades arrive. The first set showed (C-84) that QT's share price has followed its revisions, so this matters for the memo's topic on momentum.

What would move consensus. Up: fourth-quarter costs at the low end of my range (EUR 2-3 million); the catch-up of one-year renewals in developer licences; a stronger dollar (each 5% is about 4.5% of EPS); evidence of Cyber Resilience Act conversions; lower financial costs (about EUR 0.03 of EPS in 2027 and EUR 0.08-0.11 in 2028). Down: royalties; reinvestment above about EUR 5 million; slippage in the fourth quarter of 2027; a weaker dollar; a second leg down in Western automotive and industrial R&D.

Secondarily: 2028 and 2029. Consensus EBITDA of EUR 98.0 million for 2028 implies an incremental margin of 67% on 2027, above anything in QT's record (36-59% in 2022-2024), and royalties of EUR 64.2 million against my EUR 54.6 million. I expect 2028 EBITDA to be trimmed by 3-6% during 2027, as each quarter's royalties print near EUR 13 million. EPS should hold better (EUR 2.42 against my EUR 2.39), because consensus financial costs of about EUR 4 million in 2028 are EUR 2.5-3.5 million too high. For 2029 there is one broker. Nothing in consensus allows for the uses of cash from 2028 (an acquisition would add revenue and EPS before amortisation; a buyback of the authorised 8% would add about 8% to EPS) or for capacity pricing. These are sources of later revision in both directions, not forecasts.

Current multiple

D-0755
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

B+551st of 6

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the share price moves mostly on its own. Over the latest six months the dashboard's software peers explain only 21% of its daily variance (correlation 0.46, beta 0.89), and over 24 months the share explained has stayed between 11% and 32%. The level of the multiple is a different matter. QT's P/E is at the 7th percentile of its own ten-year history, but so is most of the sector: eleven of the twenty peers are at or below the 15th percentile of theirs, and QT's P/E of 14.7x is almost exactly the sector median of about 15x. So the low level of the multiple is industry-driven; the loss of QT's former premium over the sector is company-driven; and the path of the price from here is company-driven. A return to the sector's former level would come with the sector. A multiple above the sector's would have to be earned by organic growth.

Co-movement of the price.

  • Latest window (20 May to 12 November 2026): 79% of daily variance is QT's own. QT rose 21.6% and the basket 19.9%, but by very different paths: QT rose about 76% to October and fell about 16% after its third-quarter report.
  • Over 24 months the peers explained 11-16% in the periods dominated by QT's own news (late 2024; October 2025 to March 2026, after the profit warning) and 24-32% in two sector episodes: the tariff shock of April to September 2025, and the AI-driven fall and recovery of software from April to September 2026.
  • Beta fell from 1.3-1.45 between April and October 2025 to 0.8-1.0 since February 2026. The stock no longer trades as a high-beta growth share.
  • By peer, the closest are Yubico (0.48), 74Software (0.38), Sinch (0.35), Nemetschek and SAP (0.32); the most distant are IONOS (0.10) and Innoscripta (0.11). QT moves with Nordic and smaller recovering software companies more than with the perceived AI losers (Innoscripta; TeamViewer 0.29). The basket holds no automotive chipmaker or royalty company, so the first set's concern about read-across from the chip cycle (C-82) is not tested by this table.

The multiple.

  • Over six months the price rose 29.5% while consensus 2027 EPS rose 46%. The multiple fell. The whole of the rise was earnings revisions, which were QT's own; the multiple did not respond to upgrades that came from costs.
  • The 24-month-forward P/E is 13.4x, against a five-year median of 24.4x and a median of 16.2x over the last two years. The ten-year median of 29.3x was earned with growth of 20-50% a year.
  • The peers that sit in the upper half of their own ranges (Planisware at the 47th percentile on 23.4x, Yubico 54th, SAP 65th, IONOS 82nd) have visible growth and earnings-momentum ratings of A or B. What the sector pays for today is demand-led growth.

What this means for predictability. Because the price is idiosyncratic, it will be set by QT's own reports, two of which carry most of the information (D-04). The reaction to the third quarter is a warning about how those reports are read: a 7% revenue beat was followed by a fall of about 16%, with reported EBITA short of consensus because of restructuring, no change to guidance and one broker moving to Hold. Beats that come from costs and consolidation are not rewarded once they are known.

My view of the fair multiple, for the memo's topics on the multiple and the return. The model summary applies a guideline fair multiple of 43.0x, reached at the 18-month column, which gives a value of about EUR 135 a share and a return of +343%. Nothing in the three sets of answers supports a multiple of that order, and I would say so in the memo:

  1. 43x lies between the 75th percentile (39.9x) and the 90th (52.7x) of a ten-year history that was earned at growth of 20-50%. The base case grows 7-8%.
  2. The sector median is about 15x, and the closest peer in growth and margin, Planisware (growth of 13-14%, EBITDA margin of 37%), is on 23.4x.
  3. The phase is an early turn, led by costs, with the upgrade cycle slowing (I-02). On the guideline's own logic that points to the lower part of the range.
  4. There is a basis mismatch. The model's EPS is before acquired-intangible amortisation (EUR 0.39 a share, which makes it about 20% higher than EPS on the consensus basis in 2027), while the historical P/E bands were measured on consensus EPS. Today's multiple is 11.5x on the model's basis and 13.4x on consensus's. Applying consensus-basis bands to the model's EPS overstates value by that difference before any judgement is made.

A range I can support from the evidence is about 16x to 24x two-year-forward EPS on the consensus basis. The lower figure is the stock's own median over the last two years and its ten-year 10th percentile; it fits a case that stays cost-led. The upper figure is QT's five-year median and roughly what Planisware's rating implies for QT (13.4x multiplied by 23.4/14.7); it would need organic growth visibly in the high single digits. Either is a re-rating from 13.4x, and the return would still be attractive; it would be a fraction of the +343%.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the ratings map uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

A633rd of 26

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement: in European business software, outperformance comes from visible recurring-revenue growth plus margin expansion, from transitions whose exit date the market can see, and from upward earnings revisions; underperformance comes from growth deceleration combined with opaque reporting and guidance cuts. QT fits both patterns at once: it suffered the underperformance pattern in full in 2025 and is now in the early phase of the transition-exit pattern, with the exit (2027) dated but not yet proven.

Patterns from the files:

  1. The market prices the exit from a transition before earnings show it (Sector digest). QT was paid for proving its trough shallow: shares rose 76% in six months to October 2026 as Q2 and Q3 beat; Nemetschek, with a 2028 exit, was not. The Industry digest calls QT's 2027 exit "early", meaning still partly unpriced.
  2. Sell-side revisions drive the price. QT's FY2027 EPS was revised up 46% over six months while the shares rose 29.5%; when revisions stall, the shares stall. Revenue revisions were only +5% over the same six months, so the revisions are cost-led, which the market tends to trust less than demand-led revisions (the Nordic software research note of 2 November 2026 made exactly this point: Q2 beats were led by headcount cuts and licence conversion, not demand).
  3. Slow growth is punished twice (Wisdom Library): lower earnings and a lower multiple. QT's 24-month-forward P/E fell from a five-year median of 24x to 13.4x as organic growth fell to 2.6%. The reverse also holds: a visible return to high single-digit organic growth would re-rate the multiple as well as lift earnings. Planisware, growing 13-14% with a 37% EBITDA margin, trades on 23x earnings.
  4. Demand pulses revert. QT's 2021-2023 boom (distribution licences +49.5% in 2023 as customers launched programmes together) was partly a pulse; the 2025 slowdown was partly its reversion. Modelling 20%-plus growth again would be lazy extrapolation.
  5. Cost resets produce sharp margin snaps (Infineon's history; Vend's 34% to 40% EBITDA margin in a year). QT's 2027 consensus (a margin of about 30%) follows this pattern.
  6. The "AI-loser" sort (Sector digest): vendors charging per seat are punished until they change the unit they charge for; QT is half on each side.

Fit: QT fits the outperformance pattern if Q4 2026 and H1 2027 show organic growth recovering and the margin step-up arriving; it would fall back into the underperformance pattern if 2027 margins disappoint because savings are reinvested and organic growth stays low.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

B+454th of 26

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: on consensus earnings, a fair trough multiple implies about EUR 22 (-28%); in a fundamental bear case (lower 2027 earnings and a trough multiple) the shares could fall to EUR 17-19 (-40% to -45%), close to the 52-week low of EUR 18.04 and the bear valuations in the files (EUR 18-22). A takeover floor probably binds around EUR 20-22, where the enterprise value would be about 7 times 2027 consensus EBITDA.

Anchors:

  • The stock's own history. The 24-month-forward P/E is 13.4x today (7th percentile of five years); the five-year low is 11.25x; the ten-year "crash" band (10th percentile) is 16.3x. The trough multiple is therefore about 11-12x.
  • Broker bear cases. Inderes' stressed DCF (11.4% cost of capital) gives EUR 18; DNB Carnegie's 40% discount to peers gives EUR 21; its bear EV/EBIT of 11x (against peers' 18x).
  • The 2026 low. EUR 18.04-18.48, when 2026 earnings expectations were cut 30% and AI fears peaked.

Scenarios (price = trough multiple x earnings 24 months ahead, on the brokers' after-amortisation EPS basis):

  • Consensus earnings (2027-2028 average about EUR 2.2) at 10-11x: EUR 22-24.
  • Bear earnings (organic growth about 2%, EBITA margin about 25% in 2027, EPS about EUR 1.55-1.65) at 11-12x: EUR 17-20.

The takeover floor: at EUR 20 the enterprise value would be about EUR 600 million, or about 7 times 2027 consensus EBITDA and 7.5 times EBITA, against private-equity exit expectations of 15-20 times EBITDA and Schneider's 21 times EBITA for PTC. The register is open enough (free float about 66%), but a bid needs the Ingman group. The floor is therefore a cushion, not a guarantee.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

A612nd of 26

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The near-term narrative is strong and mechanical: from Q4 2026 to Q4 2027 the reported numbers will show the end of restructuring, IAR's return to growth, a roughly doubled margin and falling debt, all of which the market already expects in outline, so the narrative rewards the shares only if organic growth also improves. The novel, valuation-changing steps lie further out (new pricing, testing disclosure, capital returns) and are less well founded. The most important single date is the Q1 2027 report, when IAR is in both periods and reported growth stops flattering the organic trend; it will confirm or refute the recovery story more clearly than any quarter of 2026. For the modeller, the narrative supports the 2027 step-change and argues for building the 2028-2029 forecasts on organic growth and pricing evidence rather than on the momentum of the 2027 figures.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–64

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the two earlier sets are highly congruent on the shape of the case and on its long-run drivers, and they jar in one place that matters: the numbers for the next two quarters. The thesis (a dominant toolmaker in a cyclical trough whose 2027 earnings recovery is mostly self-help) is told the same way in the industry, advantage, economics, management, risk and forecast answers, and the forecast inputs follow from it. But the near-term figures rested on a misread consensus basis and on a fourth-quarter cost assumption that contradicts the second set's own evidence, so the "nearest testable difference" was mis-specified. Once corrected, the answers are more congruent with each other, with management's "about 30%" and with consensus than they were.

What supports and aligns. The chain from foundations to forecasts holds at each link:

  • External forces to growth: C-01, C-06 and C-80 (an organic engine running at 3-5%, a recovery confined to Europe, China, defence and medical) lead to organic growth of about 4% in 2026 and 6.5-7% from 2027. No answer assumes that Western automotive R&D restarts.
  • Structure and advantage to price: C-28, C-29 and C-45 (prices held and no rival attacked in 2025) lead to P-04's realised price of about +4.5% a year, the steadiest line in the model.
  • Economics to leverage: C-75 (85-90% of costs fixed) leads to P-01 (leverage is full within a year and shared with reinvestment across years) and so to a cone with a longer downside.
  • Management to weights: C-57, C-61 and C-69 (delivers on costs and integration, misses growth plans) lead to P-06's high weight on the 2027 margin and its discount on growth ambitions.
  • Guidance and estimates: C-68 and C-71 (floors that IAR alone nearly meets) and C-74 (a cost-led upgrade cycle from a rebased starting point) fit P-03 (most of the 2027 EBITA rise is self-help already executed).
  • Risks: C-81 and C-83 (cyclical and operating-leverage risk, not balance-sheet risk) fit P-02 (leverage below 1x even in the bear case).

What jars, in order of importance.

  1. The fourth quarter and the 2026 basis (preface, points 1 and 2). The second set argued, rightly, that the cost base held more savings than a weak fourth quarter would imply (costs excluding IAR were 11% lower year on year in the third quarter), and then set its own fourth-quarter costs 28% above the third quarter. It called itself 6% above 2026 consensus on a basis on which, like for like, it was about 12% below. This is the only incongruence that changes inputs.
  2. The contract-duration effect. The first set (C-20, following DNB Carnegie) saw a renewal tailwind from the 2025 shift to one-year contracts. The second set (P-04, P-05) dismissed it because management said in October 2026 that the mix of one-year and three-year contracts had stopped changing. I think the second set's logic is wrong and the first set's direction right. A stable mix is the condition under which the catch-up happens, not evidence against it. A customer who would have renewed for three years in 2025 and took one year instead cost QT two years of recognised value in 2025 and gives one year back in each of 2026 and 2027, when the one-year contract renews, with no change in the mix of new signings. For illustration: if three-year contracts were 30% of the developer base and 30% of those due in 2025 moved to one year, 2025 lost about 6% of developer revenue (EUR 7-8 million, the order of the 2025 shortfall) and 2026 and 2027 each regain about 3% (EUR 3-4 million). The company discloses none of these terms, so I have not put the effect in the inputs. It is an upside skew of perhaps 1-2 points on 2027 developer-licence growth, and it is the likeliest reason why organic ARR grew 11.5% (second quarter of 2026) while recognised organic revenue grew 3-5%.
  3. Royalties. The first set records management's "flat to slightly lower over the medium term". The second set models +2% in 2027 and +4% a year after, and reads that as consistent with management. +2% is defensible from a 2026 base that is already 9% lower; +4% a year from 2028 is above what management says and rests on design wins reaching production. Since the second set wrote, S&P Global has cut its 2027 light-vehicle production forecast from about +1% to -0.2%, with China, where QT's automotive strength lies, the weak region (Jefferies, 12 November 2026); against that, Infineon guided on 10 November 2026 to mid-teens automotive growth as restocking begins. I leave the input alone and treat the skew on this line as downward.
  4. The comparison with segment consensus. The second set compared its developer line excluding IAR (EUR 171.4 million in 2027) with FactSet's "developer licences" consensus (EUR 172 million) and called it "in line". FactSet's licence and maintenance lines include IAR (they sum to the group total without the separate IAR line). Like for like, our 2027 revenue excluding royalties is about EUR 4 million above consensus and our royalties about EUR 5 million below. Our revenue matches consensus only because a developer-licence view above consensus offsets a royalty view below it.
  5. Testing tools. The base case grows testing at about 15% a year (C-08, P-10). DNB Carnegie's figures (7 August 2026) put testing growth at only 6% in 2025, to EUR 36.5 million. The line was not immune to the cycle, and the unit has had no permanent head since October 2025 (C-11). 15% is an assumption about a strength that the last full year did not show.
  6. Smaller points. Growth guidance was also missed in 2023 (profit warning of 5 January 2024), so the first set's three misses (2022, 2024 and 2025) are four in four years, although margin guidance was met at or above the top of its range in 2023 and 2024. And the Nordea loan's schedule (about EUR 33 million due in each of 2026 and 2027 and EUR 76 million in 2028, including leases; AlphaSense loan report, from the 2025 annual report) means gross debt stays until 2028 unless it is prepaid (QT prepaid a EUR 60 million loan in early 2024), so the second set's saving against consensus financial costs in 2028, EUR 3.5 million, is the top of a range of EUR 2.5-3.5 million.

One figure outside the two sets jars with both: the model summary's guideline fair multiple of 43x, which I discuss under D-07.

What this means for predictability. The incongruences sat in the next two quarters, which is where the first test falls, so they mattered more than their number suggests. After the correction, three separately built reference points for 2027 EBITDA lie within 3% of each other: my build (EUR 82.4 million), consensus (EUR 84.6 million) and management's "about 30%" operating margin applied to our revenue (about EUR 84.5 million). Agreement between independent builds is evidence that the 2027 figure is well determined. It also means that the case no longer contains a clear numerical disagreement with consensus in either direction, which bears on the fourth set.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

C+552nd of 6

Layer 4 · Stock market narrative

Judgement: the dynamics that drive 2027 (the cost programme, the end of restructuring, IAR's conversion, the need for demand to recover) are fully articulated by the two brokers whose research we can see, and they are in consensus numbers. What is poorly articulated falls into three groups. First, mechanical effects that will change how the next reports look: the fall in headline growth to about +1% in the fourth quarter, the fading of revision momentum, the mixed EPS bases. Second, dynamics discussed in words but not put into numbers: royalties, reinvestment, the dollar, the Cyber Resilience Act, AI and seats, capacity pricing. Third, dynamics hardly discussed at all: the use of cash from 2028, the catch-up of one-year renewals, consensus financial costs. The share price says investors are more sceptical than the brokers: the stock is on the sector-median multiple, 24% below the mean target, and it fell after a revenue beat. The scepticism is about the quality of growth, and nothing due before late April 2027 can answer it.

A limit on the evidence. The AlphaSense report on the narrative rests on the research of Inderes (commissioned by the company) and DNB Carnegie. It holds no notes from OP or Nordea, refers to the Inderes investor forum only in general terms, and cites nothing from Twitter or X. "Who knows what" is therefore well evidenced for two of the four brokers and thinly for investors. Where it says a dynamic is "absent" from OP, Nordea or the forums, that is an absence of evidence. My first research request asks for the missing part.

Each dynamic: who articulates it, whether it is in the numbers, and where a surprise could come from.

What the share price says investors are focused on. The shares rose about 76% in the six months to October 2026 as two quarters beat and estimates rose, then fell about 16% in the two weeks after a third quarter in which revenue was 7% above consensus. Over the same six months the multiple fell while estimates rose (D-07). Read together: investors paid for the removal of the downside (the trough proved shallow and the cost base was reset), and they are not paying for upside that comes from costs. The Nordic software note of 2 November 2026 cited in the first set made the point directly: the beats came from headcount cuts and licence conversion, not from demand. The marginal investor is waiting for organic growth and remains wary of AI.

How the narrative is likely to move over 18 months. Its subject will change twice. Until February 2027 it is the size of the cost-led rebound, which the fourth-quarter report will largely settle. From April or May 2027 it becomes the organic growth rate, the question on which investors are most sceptical and brokers least precise. From late 2027 it becomes the earnings power of 2028 and the use of a balance sheet that is turning to net cash.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years. Almost nothing is articulated with numbers. Capacity pricing, the separate disclosure of testing and IAR, the effect of AI on seats and the deployment of cash are all acknowledged and none is in a forecast. Broker forecasts for 2028-2029 are extrapolations of the 2027 recovery with royalty growth added. The narrative for those years has not been written, which leaves it open in both directions.

Where our assessment differs from the narrative, and in which direction.

  • More positive than the market's narrative: the 2027 margin recovery is better founded than a sector-median multiple implies (D-02); the catch-up of one-year renewals supports developer-licence growth; cash builds quickly from 2028.
  • More cautious than the brokers' narrative: royalties; the 2028 incremental margin; the fading of revision momentum; the look of the February report.
  • The same as both: AI is a risk to the seat as a unit of price that is not visible in the numbers, and the Cyber Resilience Act is a tailwind that cannot yet be measured.

Each layer's own material

What each layer was asked, what its scores mean, and the Investment Analyst's notes and appendices

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 12 November 2026, 01:22 to 01:45 UK, Opus, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
QT Group (QTCOM-FI): answers to the first set of questions, the foundational components of the investment case

Written 2026-11-12 for the Investment Analyst who builds the operating and financial model (second set).

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Preface: the anchors and cautions the modeller needs before reading the answers

Source check. The latest Earnings Review covers the third quarter of 2026: the company published its interim statement on 29 October 2026 for the three months to 30 September 2026, which is also the last period in the FactSet financials block. The review is therefore current and is relied on below.

The shape of the company in one paragraph. QT Group sells software tools to companies that build devices with screens and embedded software: the Qt framework for user interfaces (sold as developer seats on 1-year or 3-year subscriptions, about 95% of the contract value recognised upfront), distribution licences (a royalty per device shipped, near-100% gross margin, about a quarter of group revenue), quality-assurance and testing tools (Squish, Axivion, Coco; EUR 34.5 million of revenue in 2024), and, since 17 October 2025, IAR Systems' certified microcontroller compilers (bought for EUR 205 million, funded with a EUR 150 million loan, and being converted from perpetual licences to subscriptions). It has more than 3,500 customers, none above 10% of revenue; about half of revenue comes from cyclical end-markets (automotive 10-15%, consumer electronics, industrial automation), with medical about 20% and aerospace and defence 10-15%. Revenue is about 36% in US dollars, 38% in euros and 26% in Asian currencies, while 68% of operating costs are in euros and 10% in Swedish kronor; nothing is hedged.

The numbers the model starts from (FactSet block unless stated).

  • Revenue (EUR million): 2019 58.4; 2020 79.5; 2021 121; 2022 155; 2023 181; 2024 209; 2025 216 (+3.5% reported, +6.6% at constant currency, +2.6% organic at constant currency per the company).
  • Quarters of 2026: Q1 52.7 (+11.6%), Q2 61.3 (+19.6%), Q3 51.7 (+27.0%); nine months 165.7 against 139.1 (+19.1%). Q4 2025 was 77.1 and included about two and a half months of IAR.
  • EBITA margin (company basis): 2023 30.2%; 2024 34.1%; 2025 24.0% (EUR 51.8 million). 2026: Q1 9.6%; Q2 15.1% (22.1% before EUR 4.3 million of restructuring); Q3 11.0% (21.3% before EUR 5.3 million); nine months 12.1% reported, about 18% before restructuring.
  • Consensus (FactSet median, four brokers): revenue 243 / 264 / 284 / 299 for 2026-2029; EBITDA 58.1 / 84.6 / 98.0; EBIT 39.0 / 66.5 / 79.9; adjusted EPS 1.09 / 1.96 / 2.42 / 3.11 (2029 is one broker).
  • Guidance for 2026 (floors only): constant-currency revenue growth of at least 10% (at least EUR 237.9 million) and an EBITA margin of at least 15%. Management talks of an operating margin of about 30% in 2027.
  • Balance sheet at 30 September 2026: interest-bearing debt EUR 126.7 million (EUR 120 million bank loan after EUR 15 million repaid in H1), cash EUR 50.2 million, net debt EUR 76.5 million (1.3-1.4x EBITDA). No dividend, no buyback, 25.4 million shares.
  • Price EUR 30.78 (11 November 2026), market value about EUR 0.77 billion, enterprise value about EUR 845 million; 24-month-forward P/E 13.4x, the 7th percentile of five years.

Four basis traps that will mislead the model if ignored.

  1. Broker EPS is on two bases. Inderes' EPS (1.52 / 2.47 / 2.85) excludes the amortisation of acquired intangibles (about EUR 12.5 million a year since IAR, EUR 3.13 million in Q3 2026); DNB Carnegie, OP and Nordea (0.99-1.09 for 2026, 1.81-1.99 for 2027) appear to include it. The gap of about EUR 0.40 per share is definitional, not a difference of view. The FactSet median of 1.96 for 2027 is a blend.
  2. EBITA against EBIT, adjusted against reported. Company guidance and most commentary use EBITA (before acquired-intangible amortisation); FactSet consensus "EBIT" is after it. Consensus EBIT of 66.5 for 2027 is roughly EBITA of 79, about a 30% EBITA margin. For 2026, consensus EBIT of 39.0 appears to exclude the EUR 9.7 million of restructuring (Inderes: adjusted EBITA 51.9, EBIT 38.8), so reported 2026 EBITA will be about EUR 42 million and reported EBIT about EUR 29 million; FactSet's own quarterly "EBIT (adjusted)" line also strips restructuring (Q3 2026: 7.9 against the company's reported 2.6).
  3. FactSet's gross margin line (22-57% by quarter) is not the software gross margin. FactSet classifies personnel costs into cost of sales; the economic gross margin of the licences is above 80%. Do not model margins from the FactSet gross profit line.
  4. Reported growth in 2026 is mostly not organic. IAR adds about EUR 8 million a quarter, and currency moved from a EUR 3.3 million drag in H1 to a EUR 0.3 million help in Q3. Organic growth at constant currency was about 3% in Q2 (DNB Carnegie) and an estimated 2-7% across the first nine months. The company does not disclose organic growth or the IAR revenue; these estimates come from the files and should be treated as approximate. (One digest states IAR added only 0.9 points to 2025 growth; that figure conflicts with the company's 6.6% constant-currency and 2.6% organic growth, which together imply about 4 points, roughly EUR 8 million in Q4 2025. The 4-point reading is used here.)

The one-sentence thesis these answers support. QT Group is a dominant, high-switching-cost toolmaker whose 2025 collapse in growth was mostly cyclical (Western automotive and industrial R&D freezes, shorter contracts, a strong euro) and whose 2027 earnings recovery rests more on self-help (EUR 20 million of savings, the end of restructuring and IAR's subscription trough) than on demand; the structural questions (AI and developer seats, open-source at the low end) are real but unproven, and the shares price in little recovery.
