Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: QT Group Plc (QTCOM-FI, Nasdaq Helsinki), a Finnish software-tools company reported as one segment: the Qt cross-platform user-interface framework sold as developer licences (1-year and 3-year subscriptions) and per-device distribution licences (royalties), Software Quality Solutions testing and static-analysis tools (Squish, Axivion, Coco), and IAR Systems' certified embedded compilers and debuggers (acquired October 2025, converting from perpetual licences to subscriptions), sold mainly to automotive, medical, industrial, defence and consumer-electronics device makers.

Request: T-P1

Peers: Planisware (PLNW-FR; engineering-software vendor with a similar margin structure and a recent implementation-driven margin squeeze); Nemetschek (NEM-DE; design software in a subscription transition with a dated margin exit); PTC (US; engineering software that converted to subscriptions and is being acquired by Schneider Electric); Dassault Systèmes (DSY-FR; engineering software exposed to automotive R&D budgets); Cerence (CRNC-US; automotive software with per-unit royalty revenue and cost restructurings); IAR Systems (formerly listed on Nasdaq Stockholm until 2025; the acquired unit's own margin history through its 2025 switch to subscriptions); Truecaller and Yubico (Nordic software companies with 2026 headcount cuts, for cost-programme delivery precedents); Vector Informatik, Green Hills Software and Altia (private embedded-software and tool vendors, for cost structure of the niche). Inputs: What share of QT Group's operating costs is fixed personnel cost, and how did its EBITA respond when revenue growth fell from 15.7% in 2024 to 3.5% in 2025 (company reports: EBITA margin 34.1% in 2024, 24.0% in 2025)? How much of the reorganisation launched on 20 April 2026 (up to 200 roles; at least EUR 20 million of annual savings fully realised in 2027; EUR 9.7 million of restructuring costs in January-September 2026) was already in the second-half 2026 cost run-rate, and how much will be reinvested in APAC sales and AI research, as management indicated on the Q3 2026 call of 29 October 2026? Is the EUR 3 million saving from closing IAR's embedded security operations part of, or additional to, the EUR 20 million? How did margins at software toolmakers with seat-based developer licences and per-unit royalties behave in past downturns and recoveries (2008-2010, 2020-2021, 2023-2025), with dates and figures? How long did comparable European software cost programmes take to show in margins, and how much of the announced saving was delivered? How do perpetual-to-subscription conversions affect margins during and after the transition (IAR, PTC, Nemetschek, Autodesk precedents)? Which cost lines proved cuttable at engineering-software peers (R&D at 15-20% of sales, sales, administration) and which were restored within two years? How much do Finnish, Swedish and German labour rules slow headcount reductions? How much does a change in high-margin royalty revenue move group margins at royalty-carrying software companies? Focus: How much of QT Group's EUR 20 million savings programme will reach operating profit in 2027-2028 after reinvestment, and how strongly operating profit responds to changes in organic revenue growth and royalty volumes; whether management's stated aim of an operating margin of about 30% in 2027 is consistent with peer and own-history precedents.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Planisware (PLNW-FR; engineering-software pricing with inflation indexation); Nemetschek (NEM-DE; subscription pricing uplift); PTC and Synopsys (US; engineering and design-tool pricing under subscription); Cerence (CRNC-US; per-unit automotive software royalties); Arm Holdings (ARM-US; royalty-rate increases on chip designs); Unity Software (U-US; the 2023 runtime-fee backlash as a developer-tool pricing precedent); Atlassian (TEAM-US) and TeamViewer (TMV-DE; per-seat pricing under AI and macro pressure); Altia, Rightware (Kanzi) and Elektrobit EB GUIDE (private embedded user-interface tool rivals); LVGL, Slint and Google Flutter (free or open-source alternatives); Vector Informatik, Parasoft and LDRA (private testing and static-analysis rivals). Operating conditions: a downturn in Western automotive and industrial production and R&D budgets (2025-2026); corporate seat rationalisation and a shift from 3-year to 1-year contracts; AI coding tools raising output per developer; wage and cost inflation; a weak US dollar against the euro for a vendor with euro-based costs; improving free and open-source alternatives at the low end (microcontroller and consumer devices); new security and safety compliance mandates (EU Cyber Resilience Act, Product Liability Directive, ISO 26262 and IEC 61508). Inputs: Annual list-price increases of 5-10% on 1-year developer licences and the cut in the 3-year renewal discount from 33% to 10% (DNB Carnegie): were these held, and what happened to seat counts and customer numbers afterwards? Fixed distribution-licence unit prices with no indexation and no volume discounts despite carmaker pressure: is there any evidence of royalty-rate changes at renewal or on new designs? The customer shift from 3-year to 1-year licences and minimum-seat purchasing described on the Q3 2025 call (30 October 2025): has it continued in 2026? Expert reports (2023-2024) that price increases cost QT a significant share of small and mid-sized customers and pushed developers to evaluate Flutter and LVGL: what is the current evidence? IAR's conversion to subscriptions (68% of bookings in Q2 2026 against a 40% target): what has it done to annual revenue per customer and churn? QT's planned move towards capacity, usage and compliance-based pricing, first with new customers in selected markets (Q3 2026 call): what do peers' equivalent moves show? Does Qt 6.12's EU Declaration of Conformity under the Cyber Resilience Act support higher prices or more paid conversions of open-source users? How did prices behave at the named peers in each operating condition, with dates and figures, and how do customers, competitors and sell-side analysts describe QT's pricing now? Focus: Whether QT Group can keep raising developer-licence prices above inflation while AI reduces seat needs and free tools improve, and whether royalties and the new capacity-based pricing give it a second pricing lever; how its pricing held up in the 2025-2026 downturn compared with peers.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: QT Group's revenue over 2027-2030 is shaped by several interacting loops (contract length and renewal pricing, the lag from design wins to royalties, the pricing-versus-open-source funnel, AI code volume against seat counts, and compliance-driven conversions) whose direction decides whether growth compounds or stalls. Peers: Cerence (CRNC-US; design-win-to-royalty lag in automotive software); Arm Holdings (ARM-US; royalties that follow design wins with a multi-year lag); Planisware (PLNW-FR; onboarding load against upsell and net retention); Nemetschek (NEM-DE) and PTC (US; subscription conversion loops); Unity Software (U-US; a developer-ecosystem pricing loop); Atlassian (TEAM-US; per-seat versus AI-agent pricing); Innoscripta (1INN-DE; the market's de-rating of perceived AI-exposed software); LVGL and Slint (open-source user-interface libraries); Google Flutter and Android Automotive (platform alternatives). Inputs: Contract length and renewal: with about 95% of developer-licence contract value recognised upfront, does the 2025 shift from 3-year to 1-year contracts reverse into a renewal tailwind as annual renewals carry annual price increases (DNB Carnegie's argument), and what happened to contract assets and working capital? Design wins to royalties: management cites a three-to-five-year lag from design win to mass production; which design wins of 2022-2025 (for example Chinese electric-vehicle programmes, the Suzuki e Vitara, consumer-electronics wins) reach production in 2027-2029? Pricing and the open-source funnel: do price increases reduce conversions of free Qt users and push low-end projects to LVGL or Slint, shrinking the future royalty pipeline? AI: does AI-generated code raise demand for testing (Squish, Axivion, Coco) faster than it reduces developer seats, and does Qt's large presence in AI training data make AI agents favour Qt? Compliance: does the EU Cyber Resilience Act (reporting from September 2026, full application December 2027) create a one-off conversion wave or a lasting shift? Developer community: does the size of the Qt developer pool keep reinforcing customer choice, as Hyundai Motor executives suggested about rivals' shortage of developers? IAR cross-sell: does entry at chip selection generate measurable testing and Qt for MCUs sales? Focus: Which of these loops are reinforcing and which balancing for QT Group's revenue and margins over 2027-2030, with dated evidence that each is operating now, and what would stall or reverse the two most important ones (AI and seats; design wins and royalties).

Request: T-P4

Why requested: QT Group's earnings-per-share path from the 2025 base to 2028 runs through items reported on different bases (EBITA against EBIT, restructuring, acquired-intangible amortisation, interest, currency, IAR's transition, royalties), and broker EPS forecasts mix these bases, so the inputs need to be established one by one. Peers: Planisware (PLNW-FR; net cash, low tax rate, guidance pattern); Nemetschek (NEM-DE; subscription transition and amortisation); Dassault Systèmes (DSY-FR; currency exposure of a euro-based software vendor); Cerence (CRNC-US; royalty volatility in automotive software); Infineon (IFX-DE; automotive and industrial chip-cycle bellwether and a QT silicon partner); Vend Marketplaces (VEND-NO; a Nordic company whose home-currency cost base faces foreign-currency revenue); IAR Systems (formerly listed until 2025; pre-acquisition revenue, margins and capitalisation policy). Inputs: Amortisation of acquired intangibles after the IAR acquisition (EUR 3.13 million in Q3 2026) and its schedule to 2030; whether further restructuring charges follow the EUR 9.7 million booked in January-September 2026; the terms and repayment schedule of the EUR 150 million acquisition loan (EUR 120 million outstanding after EUR 15 million repaid in H1 2026) and the resulting interest cost; the effective tax rate (21% in 2025, 19% in 2024) and any Finnish or Swedish tax changes; currency: about 36% of revenue in US dollars and 68% of operating costs in euros with no hedging (company and DNB Carnegie), and the sensitivity of operating profit to the dollar, the yuan, the yen, the won and the Swedish krona; IAR revenue (management: a decline of EUR 5-10 million in 2026 and growth from 2027); distribution-licence revenue (EUR 56.8 million in 2025; down 17.4% in H1 2026; up 18.2% in Q3 2026; management expects flat to slightly lower over the medium term) against automotive and consumer-electronics production forecasts for 2027-2028; developer-licence price and seat trends; Software Quality Solutions revenue against the EUR 100 million ambition; share count and the Performance Share Plan 2025-2027 (maximum 214,000 shares; thresholds of 2027 revenue above EUR 333 million and an operating margin of at least 25%); the use of free cash flow once debt is repaid (authorisation to repurchase up to 2,000,000 shares; no dividend paid since 2016; management's stated preference for bolt-on acquisitions); working capital under 95% upfront revenue recognition; the definitions behind each broker's adjusted EPS (Inderes, DNB Carnegie, OP Corporate Bank, Nordea). Focus: A like-for-like bridge of QT Group's earnings per share from 2025 to 2028, showing for each input what the company has said, what each broker assumes and on which basis, and which inputs (royalties, currency, net savings, interest) could move EPS most in either direction.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 12 November 2026, 07:39 to 07:56 UK, Opus, ricky login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

Against consensus (FactSet median, four brokers, 11 November 2026): revenue 243 / 264 / 284 / 299; EBITDA 58.1 / 84.6 / 98.0; my figures are 6% above consensus on 2026 EBITDA, 7% below on 2027 EBITDA and 8% below on 2028 EBITDA, and in line on revenue except a 1% shortfall in 2027.

The bases. All margins here are on the underlying basis (before the EUR 9.7 million of restructuring booked in January-September 2026 and about EUR 0.8 million I expect in Q4). EBITDA is FactSet's operating EBITDA, which is the company's EBITA plus about EUR 1.4 million a quarter of depreciation of fixed assets and right-of-use assets. EBITA is before the amortisation of acquired intangibles (EUR 3.13 million in Q3 2026, about EUR 12.5 million a year). The forecast-inputs form follows the same basis, so the Financial Analyst's model will produce adjusted EPS on Inderes' definition; to compare with DNB Carnegie, OP or Nordea, subtract about EUR 0.39 per share (EUR 12.5 million after 20% tax over 25.4 million shares).

A FactSet trap the first set did not flag. FactSet's quarterly EBITDA is not on one basis in 2026: Q2 2026 (EUR 10.7 million) includes the EUR 4.3 million of restructuring, while Q3 2026 (EUR 12.4 million) excludes the EUR 5.3 million (FactSet's Q3 EBIT of 7.9 is the company's reported EBIT of 2.6 plus 5.3). On one basis, underlying EBITDA was 6.4 (12.2%), 15.0 (24.5%) and 12.4 (24.0%) in Q1-Q3 2026, EUR 33.8 million for nine months. Any quarterly margin comparison that uses the raw FactSet line will be wrong in Q2.

Where I differ from the first set, in summary (each is argued in the answer named).

  1. Q4 2026 margin (P-01, P-06). The first set treated 2026 consensus as credible. On one basis, consensus EBITDA of EUR 58.1 million implies Q4 2026 underlying EBITDA of only about EUR 24.3 million (31.4%), down about 15% on Q4 2025 (EUR 28.7 million) although the cost base excluding IAR was running about 11% lower year on year by Q3. I centre Q4 2026 at EUR 27.7 million (35.5%). This is the nearest testable difference between my model and consensus.
  2. The EUR 3 million IAR security closure (P-03). The first set could not say whether it sat inside the EUR 20 million programme; the H1 2026 report, as cited by the AlphaSense cost report, describes it as "in addition to" the programme. Gross savings are therefore about EUR 23 million.
  3. The one-year contract "renewal tailwind" (P-04, P-05). The first set (C-20) presented DNB Carnegie's argument that annual renewals at higher prices will lift recognised revenue. On the Q3 2026 call management said there was no substantial change in the shift from three-year to one-year licences (one-year sales only slightly higher than three-year). The duration mix has stabilised, so there is no separate duration tailwind to model; the price escalator on annual contracts is real but already inside the developer-licence growth rate.
  4. ARR as evidence (P-05, P-06). Two AlphaSense reports cite "32.1% comparable ARR growth" (Q3 2026) as proof of exceptionally robust demand. That figure includes IAR, whose perpetual-to-subscription conversion creates ARR mechanically. The organic figure (11.5% in Q2 2026, from the first set) is the only demand signal; the 32.1% must not be read as organic.
  5. Net financial items in 2026 (P-02, P-08). The first set put 2026 net interest at EUR 4-5 million. The FactSet quarterly lines imply about EUR 5.2 million of net financial cost in the first nine months already (foreign-exchange revaluation losses in H1 as the dollar fell, plus loan interest), so I use EUR 6.3 million for the year. From 2027 the first set's view that consensus financial costs are too high is right and, on my debt path, larger than it estimated in 2028 (EUR 0.5 million against consensus of about EUR 4.0 million).
  6. Year-end working capital (P-08). The first set recommended 22-25% of revenue. That is the average through the year; at the year end, the definition the model uses, the ratio has been 29.3% (2024) and 29.5% (2025) because Q4 is the largest invoicing quarter. I use 27.5% for end-2026, falling slowly to 26%.

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

The company reports one operating segment, with revenue disclosed by type (developer licences, distribution licences, maintenance, consulting) and by region; it does not disclose IAR, testing-tool or organic revenue, and no margin by line. The split below is my estimate (EUR million); FactSet segment consensus is shown for comparison.

Revenue line 2025 2026 2027 2028 2029 2030 FactSet consensus 2026 / 2027 / 2028
Developer licences, maintenance and consulting, excluding IAR 151.3 158.7 171.4 184.3 199.0 214.1 developer 159 / 172 / 186
of which testing tools (Squish, Axivion, Coco), estimate about 37 about 40 about 46 about 53 about 61 about 70 not given

Group revenue 216.2 243.7 262.3 283.3 304.5 325.8 243 / 264 / 284

Margins by line, estimated: royalties carry close to 100% incremental margin and licences above 80% gross margin, so each EUR 1 million of royalty revenue moves group EBITDA by about EUR 0.9-1.0 million. IAR is close to break-even at EBITDA level in 2026 because of the subscription conversion (it earned an adjusted operating margin of 27.0% as a listed company in 2024); I assume about 15% in 2027 and 25-30% by 2028-2029 as ratable subscription revenue builds on a cost base that is now integrated. The testing tools earn above the group margin.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  • Revenue. 2026: nine months reported (EUR 165.7 million) plus Q4 of EUR 78.0 million (+1.2% on a Q4 2025 that already held 2.5 months of IAR and large distribution deals; about +2% organic). 2027-2030: developer, maintenance and consulting excluding IAR +8.0% / +7.5% / +8.0% / +7.6% (realised price about +4.5% a year on 5-10% list increases; net seats 0 to +1%; new customers, testing at about 15% a year and Cyber Resilience Act conversions +2-3%); royalties +2% then +4% a year (fixed unit prices, design wins of 2022-2025 reaching production against light-vehicle production of +1% a year per S&P Global); IAR +15% / +15% / +10% / +8% as the subscription base builds. Organic growth at constant currency about +4% (2026), then about +6.5-7% a year. EUR/USD assumed flat at about 1.15-1.16; no hedging.
  • EBITDA margin (underlying, before restructuring). 2026 underlying EBITDA EUR 61.5 million: nine months EUR 33.8 million on one basis (Q1 6.4, Q2 15.0, Q3 12.4) plus Q4 EUR 27.7 million (35.5%). 2027: operating costs +0.7% (EUR 182.2 million to EUR 183.5 million): about EUR 11 million of programme savings not yet in the 2026 run-rate and about EUR 1 million of residual security-unit saving, offset by about EUR 3.6 million of wage inflation, about EUR 4 million of reinvestment (APAC sales, AI research), about EUR 1.5 million of bonus normalisation and about EUR 1-2 million of commissions and IAR growth costs. 2028-2030: incremental EBITDA margin of 55% (QT's own 2022-2024 range is 49-59%; consensus implies 67% in 2028).
  • Quarterly phasing (Part 3). The margin step is largest in Q1 2027 (22.2% against 12.2%, the first quarter with full savings against none) and smallest in Q3 2027 (27.1% against 24.0%, because Q3 2026 already held most of the savings). Q4 carries about 32% of revenue and about 39% of EBITDA.
  • Depreciation and amortisation excludes the amortisation of acquired intangibles (about EUR 12.5 million a year in 2026-2027, my assumption of 12.0 / 11.0 / 10.0 thereafter); it includes depreciation of fixed assets and right-of-use assets, about EUR 1.4 million a quarter.
  • Net financial items. About EUR 5.2 million of net financial cost in January-September 2026 (loan interest and foreign-exchange revaluation losses in H1), EUR 6.3 million for the year; then floating interest of about 3.8% on a bank loan repaid from free cash flow (EUR 120 million at Q3 2026, gone by end-2028), less interest income of about 1.5% on cash; net income from 2029 as cash builds.
  • Tax. 21.5% in 2026 (non-deductible items), 20.0% in 2027 and 19.5% from 2028, reflecting Finland's planned cut in the corporate rate from 20% to 18% from 2027; about 0.7 points higher if the cut is not enacted.
  • Share count. 25.4 million; the 2025-2027 performance share plan (maximum 214,000 shares) will almost certainly not vest; 0.05 million a year from new plans. No dividend (none since 2016), no buyback, no acquisitions in the base case. From 2028 the base case accumulates net cash (about EUR 200 million by end-2030); management's stated preference is bolt-on acquisitions, which this model does not carry.
  • Cash items. Capital spending 0.7-0.9% of revenue (IAR's capitalisation of development was stopped in 2026). Year-end working capital at 27.5% of revenue (end-2024 29.3%, end-2025 29.5%; Q4 is the largest invoicing quarter), easing to 26% as one-year contracts and IAR's annually billed subscriptions reduce long-dated receivables. Lease payments EUR 2.5-2.9 million. Restructuring cash of about EUR 7.5 million in 2026 and EUR 3 million in 2027 sits outside these inputs because the EBITDA input is before restructuring (see the cross-check).

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

  • 2026 formal guidance (26 February 2026, reiterated in May, August and October): constant-currency revenue growth of at least 10% (at least EUR 237.9 million) and an EBITA margin of at least 15% (reported, after restructuring). My inputs: revenue EUR 243.7 million (+12.7% reported, about +14% at constant currency, since currency cost about EUR 3 million net in the first nine months); reported EBITA about EUR 45.5 million (18.7%), underlying EBITA EUR 56.0 million (23.0%). Both floors are beaten; the floors need only about EUR 72 million of Q4 revenue and about EUR 16 million of Q4 reported EBITA.
  • 2027 informal guidance (Q2 and Q3 2026 calls): "substantially better", an operating (EBITA) margin of about 30% even under modest growth; savings partly reinvested; IAR back to growth; royalties flat to slightly lower over the medium term; no revenue to be modelled from capacity-based pricing. My inputs: EBITA margin 27.8% (EBITDA 30.0%), below management's 30% because I take its reinvestment caution at face value; royalties +2%, consistent with "flat to slightly lower" read against the 2026 base; IAR +15%; no new-pricing revenue.
  • Long-term ambitions: organic growth of about 10% a year and testing revenue of EUR 100 million (from EUR 34.5 million in 2024). My inputs: organic about 6.5-7% a year and testing about EUR 70 million in 2030, a discount that reflects the misses against growth plans in 2022, 2024 and 2025.
  • Against consensus (FactSet median, four brokers, 11 November 2026): revenue 243 / 264 / 284 / 299 against my 243.7 / 262.3 / 283.3 / 304.5; EBITDA 58.1 / 84.6 / 98.0 / 107 against my 61.5 / 78.8 / 90.6 / 102.0. I am higher on 2026 because consensus implies Q4 2026 underlying EBITDA of about EUR 24.3 million (31.4%), down about 15% on Q4 2025, although costs excluding IAR ran about 11% lower year on year in Q3; lower on 2027-2028 because of reinvestment and royalties (consensus +11% a year).

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  • The company's figures. The company reports EBITA (operating profit before amortisation of acquired intangibles) including restructuring, and comments on EBITA before restructuring; its guidance margin is reported EBITA. 2026: reported EBITA about EUR 45.5 million = underlying EBITA EUR 56.0 million less restructuring of about EUR 10.5 million (EUR 9.7 million in January-September).
  • The model's basis (these inputs). EBITDA is underlying (before restructuring), equal to FactSet's operating EBITDA where restructuring is excluded and to the company's underlying EBITA plus about EUR 5.5-6.6 million a year of depreciation of fixed and right-of-use assets. Because the D&A input excludes acquired-intangible amortisation, the model's EBIT equals the company's underlying EBITA and the model's EPS is adjusted EPS before amortisation of acquired intangibles and restructuring, which is Inderes' definition (Inderes: EUR 1.52 / 2.47 / 2.85 for 2026-2028).
  • Consensus EBITDA (EUR 58.1 / 84.6 / 98.0 million) is before restructuring, the same basis as these inputs. Consensus EBIT (EUR 39.0 / 66.5 / 79.9 million) is after acquired-intangible amortisation and before restructuring: to compare, subtract about EUR 12.5 million from the model's EBIT.
  • Consensus EPS (EUR 1.09 / 1.96 / 2.42) is a blend: Inderes excludes acquired-intangible amortisation; DNB Carnegie (1.09 / 1.96 / 2.79), OP (1.05 / 1.99 / 2.42) and Nordea (0.99 / 1.81 / 2.15) appear to include it. To move the model's EPS to their basis, subtract about EUR 0.39 per share in 2026-2027 (EUR 12.5 million after 20-21.5% tax over 25.4 million shares), EUR 0.38 in 2028, EUR 0.34 in 2029 and EUR 0.31 in 2030. To move to reported EPS in 2026, subtract a further EUR 0.32 for restructuring.
  • Bridge for 2026 (my figures, EUR per share). Adjusted 1.54, less acquired-intangible amortisation 0.39, gives 1.15 on the DNB Carnegie, OP and Nordea basis; less restructuring 0.32, gives reported 0.82 (nine months reported EUR 0.15, so Q4 about EUR 0.68). For 2027: adjusted 2.20, after amortisation 1.80, reported 1.80.
  • Traps. FactSet's quarterly EBITDA is on mixed bases in 2026 (Q2 includes EUR 4.3 million of restructuring, Q3 excludes EUR 5.3 million). FactSet's gross margin line (22-57% by quarter) includes personnel in cost of sales and is not the software gross margin. Consensus D&A (EUR 5.6-6.0 million) excludes acquired-intangible amortisation, as these inputs do, while consensus EBITDA less EBIT (about EUR 18-19 million) includes it. FactSet free cash flow does not deduct lease payments. "Comparable ARR growth" of 32.1% (Q3 2026) includes IAR's conversion; organic ARR growth was 11.5% in Q2 2026.

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years

The earnings per share line is on the model's basis: adjusted EPS before amortisation of acquired intangibles and restructuring (Inderes' definition). On the basis of DNB Carnegie, OP and Nordea (after amortisation, before restructuring) it is EUR 1.15 / 1.80 / 2.28 / 2.71 / 3.09; reported EPS is EUR 0.82 / 1.80 / 2.28 / 2.71 / 3.09.

Free cash flow is operating cash flow after interest, tax, capital spending, lease payments and restructuring cash, before acquisitions and debt repayment (EUR million). In 2026 it is about EUR 5 million below what the model will calculate from these inputs, because about EUR 7.5 million of restructuring cash is paid outside the underlying EBITDA input (partly offset by lower cash tax on the restructuring); in 2027 it is about EUR 3 million below for the same reason (EUR 3 million of restructuring cash paid in early 2027). From 2028 the two should agree. On FactSet's definition (before lease payments) my figures are about EUR 34.5 / 52.7 / 67.1 / 77.6 / 85.4 million, against consensus of EUR 40.8 / 53.8 / 62.8 million for 2026-2028.

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
QT Group (QTCOM-FI): answers to the third and fourth sets of questions (predictability; guidance, sell-side estimates and narrative)

Written 2026-11-13 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Money in EUR; "underlying" means before restructuring costs; EBITA is the company's measure, before the amortisation of acquired intangibles.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Preface: three corrections to carry into the memo, and the revised base case

1. The earlier sets misread the basis of 2026 consensus. On my reading the nearest test changes from "we are above consensus" to "we are in line with it".

The first set read the brokers' 2026 "adjusted EBITA" (Inderes: EUR 51.9 million, 21.4%) as a figure before the EUR 9.7 million of restructuring costs. The second set built its nearest test on that reading ("consensus implies fourth-quarter 2026 underlying EBITDA of only EUR 24.3 million"; "my figures are 6% above consensus on 2026 EBITDA"). I judge, with about 80% confidence, that the brokers' 2026 figures are after restructuring: "adjusted" in these notes means the company's own EBITA, which is before acquired-intangible amortisation but after restructuring. The evidence, all in the files:

  • Inderes' 2026 EBITA forecast went from EUR 52.6 million after the second quarter to about EUR 52.0 million after the third. In that quarter EBITA before restructuring (EUR 11.0 million) was about EUR 5 million above the expected EUR 6.2 million, and reported EBITA (EUR 5.7 million) was EUR 0.5 million below it. A forecast that follows the reported figure down and ignores a EUR 5 million underlying beat is a forecast of the reported figure.
  • The AlphaSense report on fourth-quarter costs cites the same Inderes note (30 October 2026) as a 2026 EBITA margin of 21.4%, "an adjusted EBITA margin of 25.3% when excluding non-recurring reorganisation charges".
  • The cost arithmetic. If consensus EBITDA of EUR 58.1 million were before restructuring, it would imply fourth-quarter operating costs of about EUR 53 million, 35% above the third quarter's EUR 39.3 million; the step from the third to the fourth quarter has been 10-16% (point 2). If it is after restructuring, implied fourth-quarter costs are about EUR 43 million, a 10% step.
  • The three brokers that deduct acquired-intangible amortisation have 2026 EPS of EUR 0.99-1.09. Reported EPS for nine months is EUR 0.15, so they need EUR 0.84-0.94 in the fourth quarter, which takes fourth-quarter EBITA of EUR 31-34 million. That fits the reported basis only.

If this is right, consensus for 2026 is reported EBITA of about EUR 52 million (21.4%), about EUR 62 million (25.4%) before restructuring, and it implies fourth-quarter 2026 reported EBITA of about EUR 32 million (a margin of about 41%) and EBITDA of about EUR 34 million. The first set's statement that reported 2026 EBITA "will be about EUR 42 million", and the second set's statement that consensus expects a 15% fall in fourth-quarter EBITDA, are then both wrong. My first research request asks for each broker's basis and fourth-quarter estimates so that the memo can settle the point; the consensus the company publishes before its 18 February 2027 report will also settle it.

The same care is needed with the one-page model summary. Its lines "EBIT (Viewforth: EBITA) against consensus", "net income against consensus" and "EPS against consensus" (+41% for 2026, +12% for 2027) compare model figures before acquired-intangible amortisation with consensus figures after it. They are differences of definition, not of view. Only the revenue lines and the 2027-2030 EBITDA lines are like for like.

2. The second set's quarterly margins put too much cost into the fourth quarter. I have changed the EBITDA margin inputs.

The second set's inputs imply underlying operating costs (revenue less underlying EBITDA) of EUR 50.3 million in the fourth quarter of 2026 and EUR 53.2 million in the fourth quarter of 2027: 28% and 31% above the third quarter of each year. The record does not support a step of that size. Fourth-quarter costs were 16% above the third quarter in 2024 (EUR 36.1 million against EUR 31.1 million) and about 10% above in 2025, once IAR's first two and a half months (about EUR 7 million, my estimate) and one-off acquisition costs are taken out. The second set also treated the fourth quarter of 2025 (EBITDA EUR 28.7 million, 37.2%) as a clean base. The AlphaSense report it commissioned on exactly this question shows that the quarter carried EUR 4.1 million of IAR acquisition costs inside EBITA and no reversal of bonus accruals. Underlying fourth-quarter 2025 EBITDA was therefore about EUR 32.8 million (42.5%), on underlying costs of about EUR 44.3 million.

Three separate builds of fourth-quarter 2026 costs agree:

  • From the third quarter of 2026: EUR 39.3 million, plus the usual 10-16%, less the savings still arriving, plus a bonus true-up: EUR 43-47 million.
  • From the underlying fourth quarter of 2025: EUR 44.3 million; plus IAR's extra half-month and the end of IAR's capitalisation of development (about +2.5); headcount about 11% lower (about -3.5); wage inflation and a normal bonus (about +2); other (about -0.5): about EUR 45 million.
  • Implied by consensus on the reported basis: about EUR 43 million.

I use EUR 46.0 million, the cautious end. On revenue of EUR 78.0 million (unchanged from the second set) that gives fourth-quarter 2026 underlying EBITDA of EUR 32.0 million, a margin of 41.0% (the second set: EUR 27.7 million, 35.5%). I carry the same correction into the fourth quarter of 2027 (costs of EUR 49.7 million, 8% above my fourth quarter of 2026, which leaves room for wage inflation, reinvestment and bonuses) and leave the second set's first three quarters of 2027 unchanged. 2027 EBITDA becomes EUR 82.4 million (31.4%) instead of EUR 78.8 million (30.0%), and the level difference of about EUR 3.6 million carries through 2028-2030 at the second set's 55% incremental margin. The changed lines are in inputs-changed.md. Revenue and every other input are unchanged.

The effect on the comparison with consensus is what matters for the memo. The second set was 7-8% below consensus EBITDA for 2027-2028 and described a downside skew against consensus. On the corrected cost base I am 2.7% below consensus EBITDA in 2027 and 4% below in 2028, and on EPS after acquired-intangible amortisation I am at EUR 1.92 and EUR 2.39 against consensus of EUR 1.96 and EUR 2.42: in line. My 2027 EBITA margin is 29.2%, against management's "about 30%".

3. The free-cash-flow gap the model summary flags for 2026 (the second set's EUR 32.0 million against the model's EUR 37.2 million) is a matter of basis and phasing, not of judgement.

The second set explained the gap in advance as EUR 7.5 million of restructuring cash, less tax relief, sitting outside an EBITDA input that is before restructuring. That cannot be the whole reason, because the model takes the nine reported months from FactSet's actual cash and debt, which already include the restructuring cash paid to September. On my reconstruction the larger part is in the summary's first phasing note. FactSet's reported second quarter of 2026 includes EUR 4.3 million of restructuring and its third quarter excludes EUR 5.3 million, so FactSet's nine months hold EUR 29.5 million of EBITDA, not the underlying EUR 33.8 million. To reach the second set's full-year 25.2% the model raised the fourth quarter by 5.4 points, to about 40.9% (EUR 31.9 million), EUR 4.3 million more than the second set intended, worth about EUR 3.4 million of cash after tax. The rest of the gap, about EUR 2 million, is restructuring cash still to be paid in the fourth quarter. The smaller 2027 difference (EUR 50.1 million against EUR 53.0 million) is the EUR 3 million of restructuring cash paid in early 2027.

By coincidence the model's forced fourth quarter equals my own view of it. I have therefore left the FY2026 margin input at 25.2% and set the fourth-quarter input to 41.0%, so that year and quarter agree and the model's fourth quarter is a forecast rather than a balancing item. The FY2026 figures of record (EBITDA EUR 61.4 million, EPS EUR 1.53) should be read as on FactSet's basis: after the EUR 4.3 million of second-quarter restructuring and before the EUR 5.3 million of the third quarter. Fully underlying, 2026 EBITDA is EUR 65.8 million (27.0%) and EPS on the model's definition EUR 1.67; fully reported, EBITA is about EUR 50.6 million (20.8%) and EPS about EUR 0.97. My own cross-check of free cash flow is now about EUR 35 million for 2026 (model about EUR 37 million) and about EUR 53 million for 2027 (model about EUR 56 million); the differences are restructuring cash of about EUR 2-3 million in each year, and from 2028 the two agree. The Financial Analyst should, if the model allows it, deduct that restructuring cash in the quarters to 31 December 2026 and 31 March 2027, so that net cash is not overstated by about EUR 5 million; the effect on EPS and on the return is negligible.

The Investment Analyst's heading (Layer 4) The fourth set: how far our assessment is reflected in company guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative

All comparisons with consensus below use the reading of its basis set out in the preface (2026 figures after restructuring; EPS of three of the four brokers after acquired-intangible amortisation), and my revised base case.

Appendix Forecast inputs the Investment Analyst changed before the memo

Changes to the forecast inputs: Qt Group Plc (QTCOM-FI), as at 13-Nov-26

Money in millions of EUR, except per-share figures; costs and outflows negative. Only the changed lines are shown; every other input of the second set stands.

Part 1. Profit and loss inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
EBITDA margin (%) 25.2 31.4 33.2 34.7 35.6

The second set's quarterly inputs put fourth-quarter operating costs 28-31% above the third quarter of each year, against a record of 10-16% and a fourth quarter of 2025 that carried EUR 4.1 million of one-off acquisition costs, so fourth-quarter costs are lowered, which lifts FY2027 EBITDA to EUR 82.4 million from EUR 78.8 million and carries that difference through FY2028-FY2030 at the unchanged incremental margin of about 55%, while FY2026 stays at 25.2% because that figure now equals FactSet's nine reported months (EUR 29.5 million, which are after EUR 4.3 million of second-quarter restructuring) plus a fourth quarter of EUR 32.0 million.

Part 3. The next 18 months, one column for each of the company's reporting periods

Input Quarter to 31-Dec-26 Quarter to 31-Mar-27 Quarter to 30-Jun-27 Quarter to 30-Sep-27 Quarter to 31-Dec-27 Quarter to 31-Mar-28
EBITDA margin (%) 41.0 22.2 30.6 27.1 41.0 24.0

The quarters to 31-Dec-26 and 31-Dec-27 are raised to 41.0% because operating costs of about EUR 46.0 million and EUR 49.7 million in those quarters fit the usual step of 10-16% over the third quarter, a headcount about 11% lower than at the end of 2025 and an underlying fourth-quarter 2025 margin of 42.5%, and the quarter to 31-Mar-28 is set at 24.0% so that it rises on the quarter to 31-Mar-27 by the same 1.8 points as the FY2028 margin rises on FY2027.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of QTCOM-FI with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-12 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Peers (from the dashboard data file): Innoscripta (1INN-DE), 74Software (74SW-FR), Amadeus (AMS-ES), Addnode (ANOD.B-SE), Craneware (CRW-GB), Dassault Systemes (DSY-FR), GB Group (GBG-GB), Hexagon (HEXA.B-SE), IONOS (IOS-DE), Nemetschek (NEM-DE), OVH Groupe (OVH-FR), Planisware (PLNW-FR), SAP (SAP-DE), Sectra (SECT.B-SE), Sage (SGE-GB), Sinch (SINCH-SE), Temenos (TEMN-CH), TeamViewer (TMV-DE), Truecaller (TRUE.B-SE), Yubico (YUBICO-SE); no price history for ANOD.B-SE, HEXA.B-SE, SECT.B-SE, TRUE.B-SE

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Qt Group Plc (QTCOM-FI, Nasdaq Helsinki); a Finnish software-tools company reported as one segment: the Qt cross-platform user-interface framework sold as developer licences (1-year and 3-year subscriptions, about 95% of contract value recognised upfront) and per-device distribution licences (royalties); Software Quality Solutions testing and static-analysis tools (Squish, Axivion, Coco); and IAR Systems' certified embedded compilers and debuggers (acquired October 2025, converting from perpetual licences to subscriptions), sold mainly to automotive, medical, industrial, defence and consumer-electronics device makers

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Qt Group Plc (QTCOM-FI), what does each covering broker (Inderes, DNB Carnegie, OP Corporate Bank, Nordea, and any other contributor to the Modular Finance consensus that the company publishes before its reports) forecast for the fourth quarter of 2026 and for the full years 2026 and 2027 (net sales, distribution licence revenue, EBITA, EBIT, net financial items and EPS), and on what basis is each figure stated: do the brokers' 2026 EBITA, EBITDA and EPS forecasts include or exclude the EUR 9.7 million of restructuring costs that Qt Group booked in January-September 2026 (EUR 4.3 million in the second quarter and EUR 5.3 million in the third), and does each broker's EPS include or exclude the amortisation of acquired intangibles? What does each broker assume for 2027 about how much of the EUR 20 million of annual savings is reinvested, about distribution licence revenue, about IAR Systems' revenue and about the EBITA margin in each quarter? And what reasons did each broker (quoting OP's and Nordea's own notes where they exist), the Nordic financial press and investors on the Inderes forum and on X give for the fall of about 16% in Qt Group's share price in the two weeks after its interim report of 29 October 2026, in which net sales were about 7% above the published consensus, and for Nordea's Hold rating?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Qt Group Plc (QTCOM-FI), what valuation multiples (12-month-forward and 24-month-forward P/E, EV/EBITA, EV/EBITDA and EV/sales) did the shares trade on in each year from 2017 to 2026, and how did those multiples relate to the company's revenue growth and operating margin in each year (reported revenue growth of 28-52% a year in 2019-2022, about 16% in 2023 and 2024 and 3.5% in 2025)? What multiples do European and Nordic software companies with organic revenue growth of roughly 5-15% and EBITA or EBITDA margins of roughly 30-37% trade on today, and what have they traded on over the last ten years (for example Planisware, Nemetschek, Dassault Systèmes, Sage, Temenos, Sectra, Vitec Software, Fortnox and Lime Technologies)? What multiples were paid in recent acquisitions of engineering and embedded-software companies (Schneider Electric and PTC, Siemens and Altair, Synopsys and Ansys, Renesas and Altium, Qt Group and IAR Systems)? Which multiples, peer groups, discounts, and growth and margin assumptions do Inderes, DNB Carnegie, OP and Nordea use to reach their target prices for Qt Group? And how did the valuation multiples of comparable software companies move in the 12 to 24 months after a cost restructuring restored their margins while their organic revenue growth stayed in single digits?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Qt Group Plc (QTCOM-FI), how are distribution licences (per-device royalties for products built with the Qt framework) sold, invoiced and recognised as revenue: what share is bought in advance in prepaid blocks or volume commitments rather than reported per device as products ship, how large can a single distribution licence deal be, and what share of distribution licence revenue comes from automotive, medical, industrial, consumer-electronics and other customers and from each region? What explained each large movement in this revenue line (growth of about 49.5% in 2023, the shortfall in the fourth quarter of 2024, growth of about 26% in 2025, a fall of 17.4% in the first half of 2026 and a rise of 18.2% in the third quarter of 2026), what has the company said about large distribution licence deals in the fourth quarter of 2025 and the comparison they set for the fourth quarter of 2026, and what have Qt Group's management (which has said it expects distribution licence revenue to be flat or slightly lower over the medium term) and the covering brokers said about this revenue in 2027 and 2028? How closely, and with what lag, has per-unit software royalty revenue at comparable companies (Cerence, BlackBerry QNX, Arm, Elektrobit, Green Hills Software) followed vehicle and device production?

Terms used in the answers

Hover over a dotted term on a computer to see it written out. On a phone, this list is the reference. Terms not listed are company, product or trial names, or terms the Investment Analyst explains where it uses them.
